The line is laid out with `top_pixel_y` 0 into the window's own line allocator (`lbterm_line_quads`), whose room is kept from line to line, and kept in vectors just its size (`HeapQuadAllocator::lbterm_exact`). `HeapQuadAllocator` holds flat vertices and their hashes wherever it's used, so the Box a quad and `BoxedQuad` are gone, and the Conductor's view's shapes and `render/kept_quads.rs`'s parts copy in as one block too. Hits and misses both go in through `lbterm_apply_moved` (`lbterm_motion::content::place` and `moved`), so the hashes move with the line on every path (PITFALLS 129). What it costs Lachlan in memory: a kept quad is 280 bytes against about 130, and a full line quad cache holds about 1.6 times what main's does, about 63 MB more a window of varied lines and 127 MB more of full-width colour runs (row 91), which brings back much of what PERF.md's finding 84 took out of the benchmark's growth; that finding says the form that wouldn't (each quad's 84 bytes of fields inline, turned into vertices as they're copied in), at a cost to the copy not measured: Lachlan's call. Held by `lbterm-motion`'s tests (in the check), `wezterm-gui`'s `quad.rs` and `render/line_key.rs` tests (`lbterm.ps1 test -Package wezterm-gui`, not in the check; they passed on 2026-11-04) and `perf/line-quads.ps1` (its lone mode the one that fails with the row's fold taken out, PITFALLS 131).
   - Measured against main's build (03550aa2, main's code that day), once, `budgets -Against` with 5 rounds a build, 2026-11-04 03:52 to 04:12, the PC locked throughout (PERF.md finding 97): `busy-scroll` (Claude Code's view, every row drawn again a line on every 50 ms) the GUI thread 41.2 ms a second on main against 31.3 (lower, +-6.2), the pass 2.42 ms against 1.13, lines laid out 102 a second against 2.99, the window's private memory 419 MB against 381 (lower, +-8.5: main kept an entry a row a line passed through); `busy-lines` (a ticker under 26 lines) the pass 1.33 ms against 0.898 and the frame 2.84 against 2.4 (lower), 6.0 frames a second against 6.4 and 365 MB against 368 (higher, by 0.35 and 2.6, past +-0.21 and +-2.2); `busy-one-tab`, `one-tab` and `key-echo` the same in every metric. The frame as a whole (`busy-scroll`'s frame_ms 6.21 against 5.15, +-1.16) and the process's CPU (66.7 against 59.7 ms a second, +-9.4) weren't told apart. With the PC locked a window drew about 6.5 frames a second of `busy-scroll`'s 20 (the whole suite's rounds of 2026-11-01, the PC unlocked, drew 20 to 20.3, and `budgets.json`'s `lines` budget counts 583 to 604 lines laid out a second, every row each frame), so each frame took in about three scrolls: the per-frame figures (the pass) hold, while the per-second ones are a locked desktop's, and unlocked main would lay out about 29 rows a frame where this lays out about one.
4. **One thread a pane reads the pipe and parses, and the coalescing ends when output stops.** The pty's output becomes an overlapped named pipe (CreateNamedPipe with FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED for LBTerm's end, a buffer size of its own; the conpty host's end synchronous as now). One thread a pane reads it and parses, waiting on the read's event and the high-resolution timer together, so a batch is flushed once no more output has come for a short gap or at the deadline, whichever is first.
   - Rows: 4 (the reader thread), 6 and 7 (the loopback TCP pair: a send, a recv and two copies a read), 10 (the WSAPolls and the sleep that never ends early), 3 (the pipe's buffer, set instead of the default).
   - Saves (run 1): of a Claude-like tab's 1.47 to 1.83 ms a second of reader and 2.19 to 2.35 of parser, the send, the recv, the polls and the timer, about four fifths of the parser's and most of the reader's: about 2 to 3 ms a second a working tab, 35 to 55 ms a second of the server at 15 to 18 (estimated); 19.67 ms a second of a bursting tab's reader; a thread a pane; and the 3 to 3.75 ms every echo waits now (run 1's echo p50 9 ms). Server only.
   - Could break, and what Lachlan would notice: today the reader goes on reading into the pair's 2 MB while the parser is busy; read only between parses, the pipe fills sooner and the program blocks in its console write sooner, so the pipe's buffer has to cover a parse. A frame written in pieces further apart than the gap would draw torn: what decides the gap is Claude Code's write pattern through ConPTY, and whether its frames come wrapped in DECSET 2026 ("Not traced yet"). He'd notice typing echo a few ms sooner in every tab.
   - Size: upstream's `pty/src/win/conpty.rs` and filedescriptor (portable-pty), `mux/src/lib.rs` (`read_from_pane_pty`, `parse_buffered_data`) and LBTerm's `lbterm_wait.rs`.
   - The gap, from real Claude Code (run 5, his 12a's measurement): Claude Code needs none. Each of its frames comes whole in one console write, so one ConPTY write and one read, wrapped in DECSET and DECRST 2026, which ConPTY 1.25 passes on as written, and the parser performs it at its DECRST with no wait (502 of run 5's 528 batches). What waits on the coalescing is output outside the brackets: Claude Code's DECRQM queries, user var, title and OSC 7501 status (26 batches in 40 s), and every program that brackets nothing, a shell and its echo above all. Those come at every spacing: at Claude Code's start (ConPTY's own sequences, pwsh's, its first writes) 18 gaps between reads were under 200 µs, 6 from 0.2 to 2 ms and 8 from 2 to 20 ms (run 5's gap histogram), nothing that tells a frame's pieces from the next frame, so how short a gap keeps an unbracketing program's frame whole is still the try window's question ("Not traced yet"), and Claude Code doesn't answer it. And today each of Claude Code's frames is two batches, the DECSET alone and then the frame (row 9, PITFALLS 125): the parser redesigned here flushes before the DECSET is appended, as today's could with a line moved.
5. **A pane's rows placed on the GPU, so a scroll uploads the new row.** A line's vertices written once while it's unchanged, and each row's position given to the vertex shader apart from them (a buffer of row offsets), so a scroll writes the offsets and the new line rather than every quad.
   - Rows: 98 (3.8 MB written a scrolled frame), 104's copy, and 86's copy of unchanged lines into the layer.
   - Saves: run 4's `upload_us` of about 0.7 ms a scrolled frame and part of the submit, about 22 ms a second of the GUI thread at 29 frames, and about 110 MB a second of uploads and GPU copies while the open tab scrolls.
   - Could break: whatever reads a layer's vertices (the content hashes, kept quads, `FrameDump`) and both quad kinds in the shader. Doubtful until 3 lands, which it builds on.
   - Size: large: the shader, `renderstate.rs`, `gpu_work.rs`, `composited.rs`, `quad.rs`.
6. **The server's push read in one pass, and sent as it's held.** Partly built on perf/mux-client's flood build: the read under one lock (`mux/src/lbterm_push_read.rs`, 12's 'Built' bullet); left are the lines' copy under that lock, the cells sent as they're held and the encode straight into the outbox (rows 32 and 35). `compute_changes` takes the pane's terminal lock once and reads everything a push needs under it (size, cursor, title, an OSC 7 cwd, the mouse and alternate-screen flags, the seqno, the changed rows, the lines to send), then lets it go before any process-list ask and before encoding. `SerializedLines::from` takes links and images off a clustered line's clusters without turning it into cells, so lines go out in the form they're held. The encode writes its header and data straight into the outbox.
   - Rows: 27 (seven locks, and the cwd ask under one, PITFALLS 85), 28, 30 (compressed for nothing), 32 (cells rebuilt), 35 (three Vecs and a copy), and 63 and 81's second fetch, since no line can be newer than its push.
   - Saves, from run 2's 0.38 to 0.40 ms a push: about 80 µs of row 32, 50 of row 35's growth and copies, 15 of row 30: about 0.15 ms a push, 35 ms a second at mux-load's rate (far fewer pushes after 2). The parser waits on one lock a push, not nine. PITFALLS 86's line sent twice goes. Clustered lines are fewer bytes to compress, decode and hold; how many fewer, a codec test would say ("Not traced yet").
   - Could break, and what Lachlan would notice: the one hold is longer than each of the nine (the copies are inside it). The window then holds clustered lines (the link scan borrows their text, a match turns the line into cells). An older window decodes `CellStorage::C` already, a serde enum, so `rollback` holds. Nothing he'd see, beyond less server CPU.
   - Size: `sessionhandler.rs` (upstream's, LBTerm's `compute_changes`), `lbterm_push.rs`, upstream's codec (`SerializedLines::from`) and wezterm-surface's `clusterline.rs`, and a read of the pane under one lock in `localpane.rs`.
7. **The client thread waits on its socket alone, and the GUI's timers go on the GUI thread's own.** The client thread reads the unix domain's socket with reads of its own and takes the GUI's requests from a queue it's woken for, with no async-io reactor. The GUI thread's async-io timers (the backlog's GAP, the budget's and the slow log's wakers) become holds on the high-resolution waitable timer its message loop already waits on for paint holds (`pace.rs`, `Holds`).
   - Rows: 42 (a Recv and a listener a turn), 43 (a Box and an AFD re-arm a wait, the reactor changing hands, a hop between two threads a socket event while the `async-io` thread holds it), 54's GAP ending on the 15.6 ms tick (PITFALLS 107).
   - Saves: not known. The split of the client thread's 573 µs a PDU wasn't measured, and after 2 it reads a few percent of the PDUs. Every GUI timer stops interrupting the client thread, and the GUI's timers end at their time. Doubtful until the client thread is sampled.
   - Could break: `client_thread_async` is upstream's loop for every domain kind (TLS and ssh too), and its reconnects.
   - Size: upstream's `client.rs`, `lbterm_backlog.rs`, `window/src/os/windows/connection.rs` and `pace.rs`, the budget's and slow log's wakers.
8. **In the server, the parser owes the connections directly.** A pane's output marks it owed in each connection's `Pending` from the parser thread, ringing a connection only when it waits for news. In the server PaneOutput's only subscribers are the connections' (which touch nothing but `Pending` and their channel) and `exit_after_last_pane`'s (which ignores it). Alerts keep their task on the main thread, since they set window titles.
   - Rows: 16 (the spawn's two allocations and two global mutexes, the wake inside ON_MAIN_THREAD), 18 (the hop), 19 (the task, `Mux::try_get`, the subscribers' write lock); 21 and 22's ring stays, once a wait.
   - Saves: about 5 to 15 µs and a main-thread wake a batch (estimated from rows 16 and 19), 3 to 6 ms a second at 18 working tabs, and a queue hop off every echo's way to the window.
   - Could break: `Mux::notify`'s subscribers are upstream's to call on the main thread; a connection's end races the parser's call (its subscriber is dropped under the subscribers' lock, so that holds).
   - Size: small: upstream's `send_actions_to_mux` in `mux/src/lib.rs`, `dispatch.rs`, `lbterm_flow.rs`.
9. **Tasks and wakes joined.** `schedule_runnable` lets go of ON_MAIN_THREAD before it schedules (the scheduler set once and never locked), in both processes (PITFALLS 108). `Backlog::hand` leaves the take_all unqueued when the only PDU is the first pane's push (PITFALLS 92), and `take_first` takes the pane's items without rebuilding the queue. A spawn made on the GUI thread during a drain doesn't set the event again (PITFALLS 80). Window-level PDUs run where `process_unilateral_now` finds them. The open pane's notice reaches `mux_pane_output_event` from the take once the pane's lock is let go, without its two tasks (perf/mux-client does this for panes out of view), and the window is invalidated once a take, and not while it already waits on a paint.
   - Rows: 16, 22, 51, 53, 55, 56, 61, 64, 65 and 67.
   - Saves: for the open pane, two tasks and an InvalidateRect a push (about 36 µs, run 4) whenever more than one push lands between two frames; an empty wake a drain; the queueing of every spawner in each process on one mutex across a syscall (not measured). A few ms a second at his load, more under a flood.
   - Could break: a window's handler reached from the take mustn't run inside the pane's lock (PITFALLS 89); a task spawned during a drain must still run in that drain or the next.
   - Size: small each: upstream's `promise/src/spawn.rs` (one function) and `window/src/spawn.rs`, `client.rs`, `lbterm_backlog.rs`, `termwindow/mod.rs`, `window.rs`.
10. **What's read every batch or push without changing.** A config generation read in place of `configuration()`'s global mutex each batch and push (rows 13, 17, 33 and 59). The server's histograms with no recorder, and the run-time keys of `pdu.size` (rows 5, 15 and 35). A cli connection, and one to the GUI's own socket, subscribed only to the panes it asked about (rows 21 and 61). A pane's window found from a map, not a walk of every pane (row 20). The parser's replies to queries queued and written after the terminal lock is let go (row 13), and `get_current_working_dir` binding the cwd before `or_else` (row 27), both PITFALLS 85.
    - Saves: under a microsecond to a few each, per batch or push: a few ms a second together at his load. The last two take blocking calls out from under a pane's lock, a stall rather than a cost.
    - Could break: little; a reply written after the lock goes out after the batch's other effects, as it would from a real terminal.
    - Size: small, spread over upstream's `mux/src/lib.rs`, `localpane.rs`, `sessionhandler.rs`, `renderable.rs` and codec.
11. **A frame's new glyphs go to the atlas in one upload.** Added from perf/gpu-memory (2026-11-04), which found the cost the audit's runs didn't reach (row 90 wasn't run): each glyph the atlas takes is staged in an upload buffer of its own, 64 KB at least, so a screen of glyphs the atlas doesn't hold makes and lets go dozens of buffers and, with the allocator's blocks made smaller to save memory, blocks with them. Written in one staging buffer a frame and copied to the atlas in one go, a refill costs one upload whatever the block size, and the allocator's blocks can be as small as memory wants (wgpu's `MemoryUsage`, 8 MB) without the refill's cost. This is the flow fix to the block size, which is a value (Lachlan, 2026-11-03: "changing values does not fix the underlying issue").
    - Rows: 89 (a miss rasterised and written to the atlas), 90 (the atlas full and filled again).
    - Saves: perf/gpu-memory's atlas-refill scenario measured a screen of new glyphs 28.5 ms of 521 later with 64 MB blocks than wgpu's default (5.5%, a paired test over 20 rounds), 8 MB blocks 27% later and 32 MB 20%; with one upload a frame those costs go, and the block size, 128 MB because of them, can drop to save more memory (stage 1 measured 196 MB private a window at rest with 8 MB blocks against 366 with 128 MB, both before the view heap's change).
    - Could break: a frame that draws more new glyphs than one staging buffer holds needs it grown or split; the atlas's own growth and clear (row 90) have to take the batch with them.
    - Size: `glyphcache.rs` (where a glyph's pixels are written), the atlas's texture writes in `renderstate.rs`/`termwindow/webgpu.rs`, medium; upstream code on the hot path.
12. **A shown pane's next push waits for the frame that reads its last** (built in perf/hidden-output's second round, stages 4 and 5, measured in its stage 6: run 15; perf/mux-client's flood trace, for Lachlan's 12b, run 6, 2026-11-07). The server pushes a pane once a parser batch, about 52 lines a push under the flood, whichever window reads it (rows 25 and 26), so with the floods out of view (mux-flood's flood) and under mux-load it pushes what it did with the installed window: the 'ten times the pushes' was the window's own task, which applies each push as it comes where the installed build's took them in batches (MUX.md), and the main thread's 484 against 303 ms a second under the flood was its floods printing more in the job's 4 CPUs (PITFALLS 162). What this window changes is a flooding tab in view: the installed window fell behind its socket, and the server's wait for room let each pane's next push cover more; this window reads as fast as the server sends, so the server works out every batch's push, the shown pane's 185 a second against 57.5 frames, and 1,135 pushes a second against 712 for about the same lines (51,900 against 51,000 a second, rows carried and named stale), 511 against 335 ms a second of the main thread (the installed pair's 288). With proposal 2 holding back the lines of panes out of view, what's left that no frame draws is the shown pane's pushes between frames. So: the window says, as a frame reads a shown pane's lines, which push of it the frame has (its seqno), in the PDU proposal 2 adds to say what's shown; and the server sends a shown pane's next push only after that, the pane owed in `Pending` meanwhile as it is behind a full socket, so the push covers everything since. One push travels while a frame draws. A key's answer and its forced push (row 39) are never held, so an echo waits for nothing, and a window that never says it (an older one) is pushed as today, paced by its socket.
   - Rows: 25 (`take_next` passes over a shown pane with a push the window hasn't read), 26 to 37 and the window's 41 to 66 for each of the shown pane's pushes no frame draws; the frame's read of a pane (79) says it.
   - Saves, from run 6's counters: with a flooding tab in view, the shown pane's 185 pushes a second (68.6 ms a second of the main thread) become at most the 57.5 frames, each carrying the viewport, about 25 ms a second at rows 32 and 35's cost a row; with proposal 2 the view phase's 521 ms a second of pushes come to about 30, the flood's 474 to the echo's 1, and mux-load's 115 to about 1 and the Claude-like panes' alerts: under the installed build's 288 to 303 at each. Without proposal 2 it saves only the shown pane's part (about 45 ms a second with a flood in view, nothing with the floods out of view). Claude Code's own frames (5.5 to 14 a second, run 5) are each drawn before the next comes, so it binds only on output faster than the screen: a build's log, a big file printed, a flood.
   - Could break, and what Lachlan would notice: output that comes while a push is unread waits for the next frame's read and a round trip (0.3 to 5 ms here), so the newest line a frame shows can be up to a frame older than today's (17 ms at the median in run 6's view). A window that stops drawing (minimized, or covered and paced down) must still say it, as each push is taken, or a pane shown in it would get no more pushes; what decides that is how the window knows no frame will read the pane. One PDU on the wire, which an older server must not end the connection on (proposal 2's question too). He'd notice nothing on screen; the server's CPU with a flood in view falls by about its shown pane's part.
   - Size: the server's `lbterm_flow.rs` (a shown pane's unread push in `Pending`) and `sessionhandler.rs`, codec's PDU; the window's `clientpane.rs` and the frame's read of a mux pane (`render/pane_lines.rs`, where perf/mux-client lends the rows). Built with or after proposal 2, whose PDU it shares.
   - Beside it, on the server alone with no wire change, from run 6's rows, none depending on the window: the cwd read again at every push of a pane that prints and says none (row 27: about 75 µs a push, 15% of the busy main thread under the flood, under the pane's terminal lock), read on the `lbterm-procs` thread once the pane has gone quiet instead; an owe of alerts alone sent without the pane's changes worked out (row 29: 537 of 1,118 asks a second under `-Kind alerts` found nothing, each about 150 µs of lock waits and a cwd read), what decides it being whether anything a push carries but the title changes without output; and proposal 6's one terminal lock a push, which the waits behind the parser call for (5.6% of the main thread's samples under the flood, 26.2% under `-Kind alerts`, spinning in `Sleep(0)`).
   - Built on perf/mux-client, its flood build (stage 3, 2026-11-07; rows 27 to 29 say how): the read under one lock (two a push that sends, with the lines' copy), the cwd taken as listed and read on the `lbterm-procs` thread, and an owe of alerts alone sent without the pane read unless an alert has the push read (a title, the cwd: PITFALLS 163). Run 7: the server's main thread 490 → 407 ms a second under the lines flood, 160 → 74 under the alerts flood, 133 → 108 under mux-load; the read 105 → 32 µs a push under the lines flood, 145 → 32 under mux-load, 187 → 120 under the alerts flood, where the one lock left still waits out the parser's batch. Not built: the lines copied under the read's lock (a second lock still), the cells sent as they're held and the encode straight into the outbox (rows 32 and 35, about 300 of a flooding push's 373 µs, which proposal 2 doesn't take from a shown pane), and the main design above, which needs proposal 2's PDU.
   - Designed (perf/hidden-output's second round, stage 4, 2026-11-09, before its code; MUX.md, "A shown pane's pace", has it whole), on Lachlan's 2a of 2026-11-08, which finishes his 12b. The word goes in a PDU of its own, not in SetShownPanes' `drawn` as the paragraph above has it: `PanesDrawn` (codec, 7507, window to server), each pane of a window's open tab whose newest push applied has moved since it last said, with that push's seqno, one PDU a paint, sent at serial 0 and answered with nothing. SetShownPanes is an rpc, and saying `drawn` through it would cost a UnitResponse, a waiting task on the GUI thread and the set built again on the server each frame, close to what it saves. The window sends it only to a server that has answered SetShownPanes, which lands with it, since an older server's ErrorResponse to a PDU the client holds no promise for ends the connection. The server, once a connection has said it: a round's push of a shown pane leaves the pane unread at its seqno (`Pending`'s `unread`); `take_next` passes over an unread pane owed its changes, which stays owed, its output and alerts ringing nothing, and `wait` counts only what `take_next` would take; the word at that seqno or later ends it, and the round after the PDU pushes everything since. Never held: an owe of alerts alone, a key's answer and its forced push and every other push outside Pending (row 39), which leave nothing unread either, and the next round push of a pane given input, so an echo waits for no frame. A pane leaving the set leaves `unread`. The window's side: a word after every push of a shown pane it applies, at its next paint whether or not the paint read the pane, and as it applies a push while it can't be seen (minimized, locked, screens off: it paints once a second), so a restore draws the pane as the server has it (his 4a). Answered here: the paragraph's "a window that stops drawing" (the unseen window says at apply; a covered or paced-down one still paints) and its "one PDU on the wire" (sent only where it's known).
   - Built, the server's side (the second round's stage 4, 2026-11-09; rows 21, 25, 38 and 39 say how, MUX.md's "Built so far" what's where): codec's PanesDrawn, `Pending`'s pace (`paced`, `unread`, `input`; `take_next` passing over, `owes` and the ring leaving out what it passes over) and the PDU's arm, with the push log's `waited` and `waited_us` a pane for the measurement. No window said it until the round's stage 5 built the window's side.
   - Built, the window's side (the second round's stage 5, 2026-11-09; MUX.md's "What the window does for it, as built" has each path): a push applied is noted in two atomics the frame reads without the renderable's lock (`Drawn`), each pane a paint draws is noted just before it's read and one PanesDrawn a server goes at the paint's end (`lbterm_frame_read`, `lbterm_say_read`), so a frame with nothing new takes no lock and allocates nothing; a push no frame will draw soon is said at once (the Conductor's view, a tab overlay or a pane overlay of its own, a window that can't be seen, a push that asks for no frame); the notice goes only on a connection whose server answered SetShownPanes, the client thread's gate (`GateWatch`). Copy mode, search and quick select, which the stage took for covered panes, were neither: pane overlays that read the pane under them, so their output was paced and no path said it, and the pane stopped while one was open; the fix-up of 2026-11-09 has the frame say the pane under them (row 66, PITFALLS 246, perf/mux-pace-paths.ps1). Its first run (perf/mux-pace.ps1, this build's pair) found the server's input rule leaving a pane typed into unpaced for as long as it flooded, a word never naming a push as new as the last sent (row 39), fixed in the same stage. Measured in run 14: the shown pane's pushes under a flood in view from 222 to 254 a second down to 64 to 71 against 56 to 59 frames, the server's main thread from 141 to 161 ms a second to 48 to 52, the echo of a key the same, the last screen always drawn, a frame later at most; neither pairing with the installed build paces or ends its connection.
   - Measured whole (the second round's stage 6, 2026-11-09, run 15: the branch finished against main's build and the installed one, each in turn, twice). With a flooding tab in view (mux-flood's view, five more flooding out of view) the pane in view was pushed 55 and 56 times a second against 60 frames, where main's build pushed it 198 and 199 times and the installed one 198 and 205; the server's main thread took 65 and 66 ms a second against 419 to 433, 0.8 µs a line written against 7.2 to 7.6, and the window's client thread 53 and 68 against 672 to 717; mux-pace's single flood in view, 65 to 71 pushes a second against 210 to 254, the server's main thread 59 to 64 ms a second against 140 to 170. What it costs: the newest line a frame drew was 20 to 21 ms old at the median against 14 to 15 (mux-pace -Age, every frame between keys), about a third of a 60 Hz frame older, up to 47 against 32 at most; a flood's last screen was drawn 16 to 40 ms after it was written against 10 to 25; a key's echo into the flood the same (24 to 33 ms at the median against 26 to 40, 44 to 62 at the 95th against 41 to 76), and beside a flood out of view better (26 and 29 against 28 to 30 at the median, 44 and 45 against 48 to 53 at the 95th). The window's GUI thread spends what its frames take, which the pace leaves as they were (537 to 573 ms a second under mux-pace against 588 to 615). With proposal 2 under mux-flood's flood (all six tabs out of view, an echo in view) the server's main thread took 34 ms a second against 350 to 367, and the floods, with a server that kept up, wrote 73,000 lines a second against 46,000 to 49,000. The run first found PITFALLS 242 (a held pane's every title a real push), fixed in that stage.

13. **A title out of view reaches the GUI thread only when it changes something there** (Lachlan's 1b of 2026-11-09; designed on 2026-11-10 in perf/background-titles' stage 1 from rows t1 to t14 and run 16, before any code). Today each ◐ or ◑ of a tab out of view costs the GUI thread 4.5 tasks, 2 wakes and 0.155 to 0.162 ms (run 16), and perf/title-storm.ps1 fails its 4% of a frame of the open tab on every build since frames got cheaper. The work is three PDUs applied in a take, a task for the window's title, and the alert's two hops into a decision that, for ◐ and ◑ with no handler, decides nothing. So the design moves each piece of that decision and apply to where its inputs already are, and hands the GUI thread nothing when nothing there would change.
   - **What must still happen**, each from a row: the pane's title and folder stored where `get_title` and `get_current_working_dir` read them, before its alert is acted on (t6, PITFALLS 163); its tab's rail row repainted when the row would read otherwise (t14, `titles_move_row`); the window's title updated when the pane is in its window's open tab (the caption), or the tab bar shows the tabs' words, or a format-window-title, update-status or update-right-status handler could read it, every window running a status handler's update for any window's pane (t14, `titles::wants_update`); a taskbar button that couldn't be set tried again by the next alert (`taskbar::retry_due`); a folder alert still reaching crash restore's subscriber (restore.rs: `CurrentWorkingDirectoryChanged`; folder alerts are left as they are by this design); the GUI's mux window holding the server's title whenever Lua's `MuxWindow:get_title`, `termwindow/mod.rs:279` or the GUI socket's ListPanes asks, and not sent back (t10); the push's `Drawn::applied` and `lbterm_hold_ends` (t6); a connection to the GUI's own socket that gets the pane's changes or alerts still getting them (t7, t8); the open tab's own titles as now.
   - **Part 1, the names push applied on the client thread.** In `process_unilateral`, before `backlog.hand`: a GetPaneRenderChangesResponse with no `bonus_lines`, no `dirty_lines` and no `input_serial`, of a pane with nothing waiting in the backlog (asked under hand's State mutex: an older push of the pane applied after it would put the old title back), whose seqno, cursor, dimensions and two flags are the ones last applied, is a names push. The client thread finds the ClientPane (row 57's lookups: the mux's maps and the domain's are Sync, as `Pane` is), stores the title and folder (in the renderable under its lock, its RefCell borrowed only under the mutex, or in a names slot of their own that `get_title` and `get_current_working_dir` read), calls `Drawn::applied` and `lbterm_hold_ends` as the apply does, and hands nothing: no take, no SetEvent, no PaneOutput. Any other push goes as now. What it leaves out is t6's and t7's "no": `poll.pushed` (nothing on a unix domain), `last_recv_time` (the builder checks `is_tardy` and `since_last_response_ms`: a names push answers no input), and the PaneOutput notice, whose only takers for a title alone are the budget's news and hidden_scan, which take nothing. While a connection to the GUI's socket is open (a count its listener keeps), a names push goes the GUI way as now, so a cli on that socket still hears it. The server's names push is sent only for a pane out of view on that connection, so a pane in view takes this path only in the instant a switch's SetShownPanes is on its way, and its alert then goes to the GUI thread (part 2: the pane is in its window's open tab), whose update reads the title part 1 stored.
   - **Part 1 as built** (perf/background-titles' stage 2, `wezterm-client/src/lbterm_names.rs`): the order rule is `Backlog::waits`, a count of each pane's PDUs handed over and not yet applied, any PDU with the pane's id (a push, its hold's word, an alert), kept in hand's State and dropped as each is applied, or as the task a push that waits on the server went on to ends (`Applied::goes_on`, PITFALLS 312): the backlog's own unapplied count says applied as such a push's task starts. The seqno rule is "not behind the last applied", not "the last applied": a quiet pane's names push carries the server's read of it (PITFALLS 311), so title-storm's stand-in tabs, which print nothing but titles, send a newer seqno each title. Two rules the design didn't name: a push that would end a hold goes the GUI way (hidden_scan takes the hold's end from the PaneOutput), and so does one while local echo guesses (`should_predict`: the apply notifies for those whatever they carry). The GUI socket's count isn't needed while the alert still goes to the GUI thread (t7). Each push decided logs a debug line on `lbterm::title` (`names push pane N ... applied on the client thread`, or `to the GUI thread`). Tests: `lbterm_names.rs`' and `lbterm_renderable_test.rs`' `a_names_push_applied_here_*`, `a_push_that_changes_more_*`, `a_lines_push_after_a_names_push_*`.
   - **Part 2, the alert decided where it's decoded.** A NotifyAlert of `WindowTitleChanged`, `IconTitleChanged` or `TabTitleChanged` for a pane (folder, unseen output, progress and the rest go as now) is decided on the client thread, after its names push (the server sends the push first, so the title it reads is the new one). wezterm-client can't name wezterm-gui, so the GUI installs a decider at its start (set once, as `budget::set_waker` is), which answers from what the GUI thread leaves where any thread can read it: for each GUI window its mux window id, whether anything reads every tab's title or the mux window's (the tab bar showing tabs' words, a format-window-title, update-status or update-right-status handler: asked at each config load and override, as `lbterm_titles_read` is, an atomic each), whether a taskbar retry is due, and its rail's rows as its last paint drew them (`lbterm_panel.drawn`'s rows left as `Arc<Tab>` in a map shared with the decider, each replaced as the paint draws it, never after). The alert goes to the GUI thread, as now, when its pane is in its window's open tab, a reader or a due retry is set for its window (for any window, with a status handler), `titles_move_row` says its row would read otherwise, its pane is in no window the decider can find, or a connection to the GUI's socket is open; otherwise it's dropped with a debug line on `lbterm::title` (title-storm's counting run reads the lines), and nothing is handed. That also takes the three `has_handler` lookups out of every title (t14). Its cost moves to the client thread: the lookups and `titles_move_row` (a Tab cloned, two rows), tens of µs a title, where the client thread also reads echoes, so a key's echo can wait behind one decision.
   - **Part 2 as built** (perf/background-titles' stage 3): in the window's mux subscription, not on the client thread. The window's subscription runs where `Mux::notify` is called, and a client pane's NotifyAlert is notified by the take on the GUI thread (t8), so the alert is decided there, inside the take, before the subscription's own task: `termwindow/titles.rs`' `lbterm_title_alert_dropped`, called in `subscribe_to_pane_updates` after the mux window's title and before `budget::lbterm_hidden_output`, for `WindowTitleChanged`, `IconTitleChanged` and `TabTitleChanged` alerts alone. The decision is `lbterm_extras::titles::title_alert_goes` (pure, tested: `titles::test`'s five `*title*` tests), which goes as `lbterm_title_alert` goes: a taskbar retry due takes any title; another window's pane only with a status handler; the open tab's always; a background tab's when a title update reads every tab (the tab bar shows tabs, or a format-window-title handler), a status handler runs, or its row as last drawn would read otherwise, asked last as the dear part. Its inputs are left where any thread can read them, as the stage asked: the window's `TitleWatch` (`Arc`'d, captured by the subscription in place of `lbterm_titles_read`) holds three atomics (the mux window's title read by a handler, status handlers, every tab) set by `lbterm_titles_read_update` at each config load or override, and the rail's rows as each paint drew them, the same `Arc<HashMap<TabId, Tab>>` the paint keeps as `lbterm_panel.drawn` (one `Arc` and a mutex a paint, no copy), with the idle timer's length it drew with; the taskbar's failed-button retries moved from a thread-local to a process-wide `Mutex<Retries>` (`taskbar.rs`, `FAILED`). Mux, tabs and panes are Sync. So it reads nothing of the GUI thread's own, and runs as well on the client thread if the alert is ever notified there. The row's check got a shortcut (`lbterm_panel::claude::titles_read`, what `row_since` reads of the titles, which it now reads them through: the tab's title stripped, the active pane's program name, the first Claude glyph): titles that read the same, as ◐ and ◑ do, answer no with no Tab cloned and no row made (`titles_a_row_reads_the_same_never_make_another_row` checks the shortcut against the full compare on every pair that reads the same of 3 tabs, 3 tab titles and 10 pane titles: hooks, none, and a link). What it saves: t12 to t14 for a title that changes nothing, a task, a hop's task and the window's event (about 50 µs and two tasks a title in run 16); the take that carries the alert (t3 to t5, t8) still runs, until the alert is decided before `backlog.hand` as this item first designed, which needs the GUI socket's count (t7) and every other subscriber of a NotifyAlert checked for the client thread (frontend's, restore's, the client domain's, `lbterm_parked`'s, the GUI socket's connections). The has_handler step of "Steps that stand on their own" came with it. title-storm's counting window counts `dropped in the subscription` lines and fails a titles storm whose title alert reaches the window's task to find nothing to do (`title pane N window W: nothing shown changed` without it).
   - **Part 3, the mux window's title.** WindowTitleChanged needs no task of its own (t9, row 56). With parts 1 and 2 it's all a background title would still hand over, so the backlog keeps a window's latest title from the server (as `Pending` does on the server) and the next take that runs for anything else applies it inline; it queues a take of its own only when the decider says its window has a handler that reads titles, which is when the window's subscription lets `WindowTitleChanged` through today. The readers that ask (Lua's `MuxWindow:get_title`, `termwindow/mod.rs:279`, the GUI socket's ListPanes) apply what waits first, through a ClientDomain call on the GUI thread, so each reads the server's latest. Rejected: the client thread setting the mux window's title itself, since `Window::set_title` notifies the subscribers on the calling thread and the GUI's are written for the GUI thread (the budget is thread-local).
   - **Parts 2 and 3 on the client thread, as built** (perf/background-titles' fix-up, after the review found the take and the window title's task left and title-storm passing only by exempting `client.rs`: PITFALLS 317 and 319). In `process_unilateral`, after `lbterm_names::applied_here` and before `backlog.hand`, `lbterm_titles::decided_here` (wezterm-client) takes the other two PDUs. A NotifyAlert of a title, icon title or tab title, of a pane with nothing waiting (`Backlog::waits`: its names push, and anything older of it, already applied), is looked up (the mux, the ClientDomain, `remote_to_local_pane_id`, no resync) and asked of the GUI's `Decider`, which wezterm-gui installs when its first window subscribes (`titles::lbterm_watch_window`, called from `subscribe_to_pane_updates`, registers each window's `mux_window_id`, `TitleWatch` and dead flag in `WATCHED`): dropped when every live window's `drops` (`lbterm_title_alert_dropped`'s decision, unchanged) says so and no connection to the GUI's own socket is open (`dispatch::lbterm_connections`, a count `process_async` keeps from before its subscription until it ends), with a debug line (`title pane N decided in U us: nothing shown changed, dropped on the client thread`). Nothing is notified: of the GUI's other subscribers of an alert, frontend's acts on toasts, bells and progress, crash restore's on folders and `claude_session`, the client domain's and `lbterm_parked`'s on no alert, and a GUI-socket connection's (`Pending::alert`) is why the count stops the drop. Anything else goes to the backlog as before, where the subscription decides it again on the GUI thread. The mux window's WindowTitleChanged is kept (`ClientDomain::lbterm_window_title_kept`: `remote_to_local_window`, `server_titles.heard` so it isn't sent back, then mux's `lbterm_window_titles::keep`, the newest a window), and handed as well only while the Decider says something reads a mux window's title as it changes (any window's `TitleWatch::window_title_read`, or a GUI-socket connection, or no window yet); every take then sets what's kept on its window at its end (`lbterm_titles::set_kept`, on the GUI thread, where `Window::set_title`'s notification belongs), and the four readers read the kept title first (`lbterm_window_titles::title`: Lua's `MuxWindow:get_title`, the tab information's `window_title`, the GUI socket's ListPanes, the client domain's send-back check; PITFALLS 322). A handed title's task forgets the kept one if it's still the same (`set`). Without a Decider (a process with no GUI, a test) everything goes to the GUI thread as before. What the client thread pays, said on each line while `lbterm::title` is at debug and title-storm's counting window prints its medians (run 20): a names push's apply, a title alert's decision (the lookups, `WATCHED`, each window's `get_window` scope and tab walk, the watch, the retries, `titles_move_row`'s `titles_read` compare and each of the tab's panes' `get_title`), a window title's keep (a map insert under a mutex). The locks the decision takes and the rule that keeps it from deadlocking are PITFALLS 321. The Saves line holds on a mux domain: no PDU handed, no take, no wake, no task for a background title that changes nothing (run 20); `Drawn`'s atomics, now written by both threads, were made safe for it (PITFALLS 318). Tests: `lbterm_titles`' `only_a_title_icon_title_or_tab_title_is_decided`, `lbterm_window_titles`' `a_kept_title_is_read_until_it_is_set_and_a_newer_one_outlives_an_older_set`, titles.rs' `with_no_window_the_decider_drops_no_alert_and_keeps_no_window_title`; the decision itself is stage 3's, tested there; title-storm holds the rest in a window.
   - **Saves:** with no handler, no connection to the GUI's socket and ◐ or ◑ in a tab out of view, no PDU handed, no take, no wake and no task: from 4.5 tasks, 2 wakes and 0.155 to 0.162 ms of the GUI thread a title (run 16) to none, about 1.3 ms of each second with Lachlan's 8 working tabs, and title-storm, which holds a title by counts since stage 4 ("Held by counts", below), then holds every site to none. A title that moves a row, or with a handler: as now, plus the decision on the client thread. The server's side is unchanged (0.37 ms a title in run 16, its parse included).
   - **Could break, and what Lachlan would notice:** a rail row that keeps an old title (a decider reading a row older than the one on screen: the paint leaves its copy as it draws); the caption not following the open tab's title (the open tab is read from the mux, which a switch changes on the GUI thread: a title decided out of view as its tab is switched to is shown by the switch's own update, which reads the title part 1 stored); an older title put back by an older push (part 1's backlog check); a cli on the GUI's socket missing a title (the count); Lua reading a stale mux window title (part 3's readers).
   - **Size:** wezterm-client's `client.rs` (`process_unilateral`), `clientpane.rs` and `renderable.rs` (the names apply), `lbterm_backlog.rs` (the pane-waiting check, a window's title kept), and a module of its own for the decider's hook; wezterm-gui's `termwindow/titles.rs` (the readers published), `render/tab_panel.rs` (the drawn rows shared), `termwindow/mod.rs` (the decider installed), lua-api-crates/mux's `window.rs` and the GUI socket's ListPanes (the window's title applied first). Upstream files: `client.rs`, `clientpane.rs`, `renderable.rs`, the Lua crate. Medium.
   - **Steps that stand on their own**, each checked with `title-storm.ps1 -Metrics`: the window's title applied inline in the take (t9, about 7 µs and a task a title); the three `has_handler` lookups answered at config load (t14); the alert's two hops made one (t12, row 64).
   - **Held by counts** (perf/background-titles' stage 4, 2026-11-10): `perf/title-storm.ps1` holds this path by the titles phase's GUI-thread tasks, by the line that queued each (PITFALLS 310), against its rest phases', no longer by its 4% of a frame, which failed on every build. As built (parts 1 and 2), a background title on a mux domain runs the client's take (1.39 a title in run 17, 44 µs each) and the mux window title's task (one a title, 10 µs), which the script leaves by their file (`wezterm-client/src/client.rs`, its `$leftSites`), and nothing of the window's, which it holds to none, with any other site that runs once in ten titles. The GUI thread still woke twice a title (169 wakes for 84 titles) and spent 0.162 ms a title in that run; run 16's 0.155 and 0.162 on main aren't a before for it, being another run (PITFALLS 320: main's code read 0.204 to 0.243 in run 19's interleaved rounds), and nor is its time outside tasks. The before and after (run 18: five rounds of the installed build and the branch's in turn) says the same: the GUI thread's cycles a title less the metrics' task time a title, 0.073 ms on the installed build and 0.097 on the branch's (medians; +0.023 +-0.018 at 99%, higher), on the mux domain only (`-Local` 0.074 against 0.065, the same), against the installed copy; against main's slot build in run 19 it was +0.017 +-0.045, the same, so it isn't established (PITFALLS 320), and a stack sample over the titles phase is still the way to split the GUI thread's time outside tasks; the wakes didn't move (1.98 a title on both). Not found: no message, timer or frame in the phase, and the take ran 1.45 times a title against 1.30 (+0.13 +-0.12), each at 44 µs against 54. Run 19, against main's slot build, puts the client's takes at 2.21 to 2.49 a title (+0.27 +-0.18, higher), and they are part 1's (PITFALLS 317): with the names push no longer handed, a title's first PDU in the backlog is its alert, so the take it queues can run before the mux window's title comes and a second take is queued for that; and the time outside tasks is the GUI thread's wake and the executor's own work round each task, which a stack sample over the titles phase would split (the metrics time a task, not what wakes it). Run 18 also puts what parts 1 and 2 saved at 35 to 40 µs of the GUI thread a title (0.205 to 0.170 ms on the mux domain, 0.179 to 0.140 with `-Local`) and 1.7 to 1.8 tasks a title. So parts 1 and 2 alone didn't meet the rule this item was built for (no task, no take and no wake for a title that changes nothing): on a mux domain the take, the window title's task and two wakes a title were left, and part 1 on its own was about a wash on the GUI thread, its cheaper takes paid for by more of them. The Saves line above counts on no PDU handed and no wake, which the fix-up built ("Parts 2 and 3 on the client thread, as built"); title-storm's `$leftSites` is empty on a mux domain since, every site and the wakes held to none, with exact counts from the counting window's lines (PITFALLS 319). With the window's own panes (`-Local`, which this design doesn't cover) a background title is one `deliver_held` task (`mux/src/localpane.rs`: the alert, which the subscription then drops), one output notice (`mux/src/lib.rs`, `send_actions_to_mux`'s task) and the window's hop and event task for that output (`mod.rs`'s subscription spawn and `window.notify`), since `budget.rs`'s `lbterm_hidden_output` takes only a ClientPane's output out of the open tab with no task: a local pane's output out of view, every batch of it, costs the window two tasks more, which taking a local pane's there too would end. Why it takes only a client pane's isn't traced; title-storm `-Local` leaves the two window sites at once a notice (before stage 3 they ran twice a title, the alert's once more).

Left as they are: the title and cwd in every push (doubtful, row 31: sending them only on change is a wire change for a µs or two a push), zstd (row 35: about 8% of a push for pushes 29 times smaller), the atlas cleared when full (row 90: perf/gpu-memory's ground), and DWM's work a present (row 101: not measured).

### The runs

Each was one profiling run on the slot's release build of main (with its .pdb files), in a warden GPU job of 4 CPUs, its test windows and servers handed to the job.

- **Run 1** (segment 1), 2026-11-04 00:02 to 00:04, after 16 minutes in the GPU queue: `perf/mux-load.ps1 -Measure -Claude 6 -Burst 1 -Seconds 50`. A test mux server of its own, 120x40 panes, an echo tab in view, six Claude-like stand-ins out of view (the alternate screen with a 32-row scroll region, a status block in 256-colour SGR with two 120-column rules, an answer line every other frame; `Thread.Sleep(50)`, which Windows' 15.6 ms tick makes about 16 frames a second, each about 962 B in about 2.5 console writes), and one burst tab (40 lines of 60 x's about 65 times a second, 2,440 B in about 10 writes). Six seconds after the 8 conpty hosts were up, a 10.06 s span with nothing sampling: every server thread's QueryThreadCycleTime and each process's QueryProcessCycleTime and GetProcessIoCounters, cycles turned into ms at the measured 2.993 GHz. Server: 113.73 ms of CPU a second; 864.26 reads a second (248.6 KB/s, the ReadFiles on the 8 pipes), 6.36 writes (keys into the echo's input pipe), 602.91 others. Its threads in ms a second: main 60.27; the burst tab's reader 19.67 and parser 9.03; the Claude-like parsers 2.19 to 2.35 and their readers 1.47 to 1.83; `async-io` 1.01; the echo pane's three 0.40, 0.36 and 0.14; `lbterm-keep-socket` 0.03. Conpty hosts (CPU ms a second, writes a second, KB a second, bytes a write): the echo's 0.72, 6.36, 0.01, 1.0; the six Claude-like 1.60 to 1.69, 40.25 to 40.54, 15.38 to 15.49, 391.0 to 391.7; the burst's 16.34, 642.95, 155.72, 248.0. Then `perf/sample.ps1 -ProcessId <the server>` on its 16 busiest threads, 15 s asked at 250 Hz, which gave 811 samples a thread (about 54 rounds a second): the main thread 94.3% in ZwWaitForAlertByThreadId under flume's recv in `SimpleExecutor::tick`, 5.8% running tasks (`lbterm_flow::fill` → `push_pane_changes` 5.2%, `compute_changes` 2.7%, `Pdu::encode` 2.3%, `ProcShared::list` 0.4%, QueryInformationJobObject 0.2%); the Claude-like parsers 93.7 to 94.7% in recv, 5.2 to 6.0% in `Timer::sleep`, 0 to 0.3% in `perform_actions`, `flush_print`, `set_cell_impl`, `perform_csi_edit`, `clear_line` and WSAPoll, one sample in `spawn_into_main_thread` → flume's send → NtAlertThreadByThreadIdEx; the burst parser 84.8% recv, 15.0% `Timer::sleep`, 0.1% perform (`Screen::scroll_up`, `Line::new`); the Claude-like readers 99.8 to 100% NtReadFile, the burst reader 98.5% NtReadFile and 1.5% send; `async-io` 99.9% GetQueuedCompletionStatusEx. mux-load's own report over its 48 s: the server 120.4 ms of CPU a second (main thread 63.2), 144.6 page faults a second, 857.3 reads, 6.4 writes, 606.5 other I/O a second, 37.3 MB private (peak working set 47.3); the window 263.6 ms a second (GUI thread 176.1); 320 keys, the echo p50 9 ms, p95 16, max 79; 8 tabs. The job exited 1 only because its last snapshot came after the test had ended its server; the per-program counters were lost with it (PITFALLS 98), so the program side is worked out from the stand-ins' code and ConPTY's bytes. Beside it, not a run of LBTerm: a loopback TCP probe in .NET with the socketpair's options (row 6).
- **Run 2** (segment 2), 2026-11-03: `perf/mux-load.ps1 -Seconds 30 -Measure -Count` (six Claude-like stand-ins, two tabs printing 40 lines every 5 ms, the echo tab in view with a key every 150 ms, 120x40). The warden paused it twice ("the GPU is busy outside the queue") and resumed it; a first try was paused before its window opened and cancelled having measured nothing. Beside it `perf/sample.ps1 -Exe lbterm-mux-server -StartAfterSec 14 -PickSec 2 -Seconds 15 -Threads 8 -Hz 1000 -Depth 64 -Top 60`: 475 samples a thread (about 31 a second each); with the pauses, the 15 s aren't known to lie wholly inside the measured 30, though the readers were busy at the pick. The load phase (30.09 s): the server 74.2 ms of CPU a second (main thread 39.4), 269 page faults a second, 662.6 reads, 3.0 writes and 384.8 other I/O a second, 45.4 MB private; the window 162 ms a second (GUI thread 103.7), 666.8 MB private; 89 keys, the echo p50 10 ms, p95 17, max 34; all 9 tabs reached the window. The count phase (codec=debug, 30.07 s): the server sent 3,292 PDUs (109 a second, 36.9 KB a second): 3,097 pushes (103 a second, 1,132,039 B, 365 B each), 82 UnitResponse, 82 LivenessResponse, 31 GetLinesResponse, 3,123 compressed and 5 tried and sent as they were; the window sent 195 (82 SendKeyDown, 82 GetPaneRenderChanges, 31 GetLines), none compressed; the server 74.4 ms a second, the main thread 41, the echo p50 10, p95 15. So 0.38 to 0.40 ms of the main thread a push, as MUX.md's earlier run of the same script on perf/mux-server (86 to 88 ms a second for about 238 pushes, 0.36 to 0.37 ms). The main thread's 475 samples: 451 parked in flume's recv, 24 busy, all inside `RawTask::run`: `fill` 23, of them `lbterm_push`/`push_pane_changes` 22; `Pdu::encode` 11; `compute_changes` 11 (`SerializedLines::from` 5, the proc list 4, `lines_to_send` 2); one sample each of `RawTask::schedule`, `HashMap::retain`, `drop_in_place<metrics::Key>` and a BTreeMap IntoIter; no parking_lot `lock_slow`. The other server threads sampled: two readers 99% in NtReadFile (0.6 to 0.8% in send), five parsers 77 to 95% in recv and 5 to 22% in the coalescing timer, about a sample each of parsing. The window's `metrics` wasn't read.
- **Run 3** (segment 3), 2026-11-03 at 23:48, after about 10 minutes queued: `perf/mux-load.ps1 -Claude 6 -Burst 2 -Seconds 60 -Measure`, the window drawn as Lachlan's by `New-TestConfig` (his hyperlink rules, a scrollback of 50,000, the compositor on). The window's metrics were reset 5 s after its nine tabs settled and read 10.03 s later, with nothing sampling it. Received: 2,404 GetPaneRenderChangesResponse (864,401 B), 65 LivenessResponse, 65 UnitResponse, 2 GetLinesResponse, no alerts; sent 65 SendKeyDown, 65 GetPaneRenderChanges, 2 GetLines. The client thread 1,453 ms of CPU; the GUI thread 2,109 ms (21%), busy 2,357 ms and waiting 7,673, 1,786 wakes; 6,899 tasks for 2,159.6 ms and 202 messages for 30 ms; 65 frames whose paint parts took 107 ms (pass 40, draw 43, present 24). The tasks by where they're spawned: `process_unilateral`'s take_all 1,748 runs for 2,131.7 ms (mean 1.22 ms, max 7.6) and take_first 130 for 6.1 ms (mean 47 µs, max 0.22 ms); `subscribe_to_pane_updates`' hop 2,345 for 5.82 ms (2.5 µs, max 21); `mux_pane_output_event_callback`'s `window.notify` hop 2,345 for 8.56 ms (3.65 µs, max 83); the poll's 126 for 2.7 ms; the fetch's 4 for 0.23 ms; `key_down`'s SendKeyDown task 130 for 1.5 ms; `set_text_cursor_position` 65 for 2.6 ms. 2,345 PaneOutput notices (235 a second) and 65 invalidates, all from `mux_pane_output_event`, giving 65 frames. `executor.spawn_delay` mean 0.499 ms, p50 0.025, p95 2.06, p99 3.70, max 7.73; `rpc` get_pane_render_changes p50 0.29 ms (p95 1.19), get_lines 2.56 and 4.85 ms, key_down p50 0.52; the budget's key echo over 65 keys p50 8.8 ms, p95 12.7, max 15.5. The sampler missed: pointed at the first `lbterm-gui` whose command line named the test's class, which wasn't the test's window (PITFALLS 97), it took no stacks. The warden then paused the job ("the GPU is busy outside the queue") and the second metrics read timed out; mux-load's own 60 s summary covers that paused span, so it isn't a measure.
- **Run 4** (segment 4), 2026-11-03 23:45 to 23:47, after about 11 minutes queued for the GPU and the CPU's heat: `perf/paint-cost.ps1 -Measure -Runs 4 -Seconds 10`, a window like Lachlan's (his look, 1080 by 1920, three tabs on a test mux domain, the Conductor's outlines, the open tab printing a coloured line of about 90 characters every -IntervalMs 16, each scrolling the screen), beside it `perf/sample.ps1 -Class <the test's> -StartAfterSec 14 -Seconds 20 -Threads 5 -Top 80`, and a thread reading `metrics` every 3 s without a reset (paint-cost resets at each run's start). Lachlan was at the PC: Chrome's popups failed paint-cost's window check six times, which judges nothing in -Measure. The sampler covered the warm-up's end, run 1 and part of run 2 and slowed their wall times (the pass 13.6 and 10.4 ms a frame) but not the GUI thread's cycles (9.9 and 9.4 ms), so the paint's parts are from runs 3 and 4 and the shares from the sample. paint-cost's runs, 330 frames each at about 29 a second, a push a frame: the GUI thread 9.90, 9.41, 9.90 and 9.31 ms a frame; the process 11.95, 11.26, 11.80, 11.18; the pass 13.55, 10.43, 7.67, 7.21; the draw 2.71, 2.01, 1.34, 1.27; present 1.41, 0.68, 0.43, 0.37; visuals drawn 1.71, 1.25, 1.13, 1.07 a frame (kept 21.3 to 21.9 of 23); uploads 4.4 to 5 a frame, 3,754 to 3,772 KB a frame; nothing made or built; `upload_us` 1,637, 1,164, 793, 749, `record_us` 326, 186, 144, 137, `submit_us` 373, 379, 239, 229; GPU dedicated 470 MB, private 640 to 650 MB. Metrics over run 4's 11.3 s, unsampled: 330 frames for 330 requests; the GUI busy 2,799 ms and waiting 8,498, 671 wakes; the GUI thread 3,078 ms of CPU (27.2%), the process 3,781 (kernel 625); the paints' pass 2,378 ms, draw 419, present 121 (8.85 ms a frame; `gui.paint.impl` mean 8.845, p50 8.32, p95 11.86, max 18.87); the caches: line_quad 687 hits and 29,673 misses and evictions, line_state 0 hits and 30,360 evictions, line_to_ele_shape 29,356 hits and 317 misses, shape 7,003 hits and 290 misses, glyph 1,414 hits and no miss; WM_PAINT 340 messages for 58.5 ms of their own; the tasks: the push task 329 (42.3 ms), the notice's dispatch 329 (12.0 ms), the poll task 656 (11.2 ms), the subscriber's 329 (1.5 ms), `executor.spawn_delay` mean 1.79 ms, p95 9.83, max 18.9; GetPaneRenderChanges sent 328, LivenessResponse 328 and 329 pushes received (272 bytes compressed on average), the poll's round trip 0.37 ms mean; the threads: main 3,078 ms, `lbterm-state` 453, `notify-rs` 141, `async-io` 47, "the client reactor" 31, two unnamed 15.6 each; the atlas 1024 with no clear or growth. Runs 1 to 3 said the same counts. The sample: 2,856 of the GUI thread over about 20 s (about 143 a second with five threads), 62.7% waiting in MsgWaitForMultipleObjects and 35.6% in `wm_paint`; its shares, scaled to run 4's 8.85 ms paint, are in rows 79 to 102: the rows 6.2 ms (laying out again 3.0, applying 1.75, evicting 1.0, hashing 0.5), the draw and present 1.6 to 1.9; outside the paint `run_windows` 0.7%, PeekMessage 0.6%, `process_unilateral_now` 0.3%.

- **Run 5** (real Claude Code's output, 2026-11-04 02:10, perf/line-quads-by-content's first stage): `perf/claude-output.ps1` on that branch's build (f21154d52's tree, copied with its conpty.dll and OpenConsole.exe, which the pane's conpty host ran), in a warden GPU job of 4 CPUs holding the measurements' lock: Claude Code 2.1.317 (fullscreen, a named conversation) against the stand-in API on a loopback port, the first tab of a mux domain of the test's own, in a window of 1080 by 1920 drawn as Lachlan's (the pane 106 by 92), LBTERM_PTY_RECORD set for the window and so its server (`mux/src/lbterm_ptyrec.rs`). A long answer of 160 lines streamed in 444 text deltas over 13.9 s; a turn of 10 Read calls, each answered 400 ms after its request (5.1 s); a turn held 8.3 s with only the spinner; then a probe tab writing DECRQM 2026 and two synchronized frames. The PC was locked throughout (LogonUI; nothing recorded depends on what's drawn), other work took 0.125 of the job's CPUs and the PC was 18.6% busy, the job's clock at 1.07 of nominal. Analysed by `bench/pty-frames.ps1` (`mux/tests/lbterm_pty_frames.rs`): 301 reads of Claude Code's pane in 39.9 s, 251 frames all bracketed, each one read. A first run of the script without the tool turn, at 02:01, said the same of the stream, the spinner and the probe (167 frames, all bracketed in one write; 100 streaming frames, 88 B at the median; 44 spinner frames, 65 B); its own check failed on a folder listing's size (PITFALLS 126), not on what it recorded.

Two runs before them (02:48 and 02:50) failed on the script's own `$t` (PITFALLS 245) with the same counts; the first had another session's test windows (perf-tab-switch, perf-cost) come up from 02:48:31, during or after its titles phase, and 0.181 ms a title.

The PC unlocked, no input for 4,143 s at the start and none through it, so `Get-DisplaysAsleep` said the displays slept, yet each run's draw phase painted 101 to 103 frames in 10 s; the job's CPUs 35 to 43% busy over each run, the test's own processes included (`Start-PcWatch` round the run, the first run's clock unread: PdhGetFormattedCounterArray 0x800007d6), the whole PC 17 to 32%, the clock 2.54 to 2.85 GHz; nothing else held `lbterm-measure`. Unchanged on both domains: `repeat` (the same title again every 100 ms: 83 against 88 µs a title on the mux domain, 84 against 79 with `-Local`), `mix` (1.84 against 1.85 ms a title, its OSC 7501 reports painting 35 to 37 frames), and a frame of the open tab (3.69 against 3.58 ms, 4.04 against 3.72 with `-Local`). `lbterm.ps1 budgets -Only busy-tabs,busy-rail-open,twelve-tabs -Against` the same copies, queued straight after, was refused for the sleeping displays (exit 2, PITFALLS 239).
