Per title less the first rest phase, main's rounds then the branch's, then the difference of the means and its +-99% half width: the GUI thread 0.230, 0.218, 0.217, 0.222 ms against -0.007, -0.004, -0.006, -0.005 (-0.227 +-0.016, lower); tasks 4.13 to 4.26 against -0.02 (-4.20 +-0.18, lower), their time 0.139 to 0.148 ms against nothing (-0.146 +-0.011); the client's 2.35 to 2.48 against 0, the window's 1.79 to 1.83 against 0.02; wakes 1.93 to 1.98 against -0.02 (-1.98 +-0.07, lower); the time outside tasks 0.076 to 0.082 ms against 0 (-0.081 +-0.006, lower); the server 0.504 against 0.498 ms (same); the GUI process -0.415 +-0.434 (same: its other threads swing more than a title, the client thread's 31 µs included); `repeat` 89 µs a title against none (-91 +-13, lower); `mix` 2.12 against 1.83 ms a title (-0.26 +-0.21, lower); a frame of the open tab 4.05 against 4.06 ms (same); rest the same. So a background title that changes nothing now costs the GUI thread nothing measurable, no task and no wake, where main's costs 0.22 ms, four tasks and two wakes: about 1.8 ms of each second with eight working tabs. The client thread pays about 31 µs a title for it (6 + 16 + 9), the thread that reads key echoes, so an echo that arrives with a title can wait up to that much behind it (none measured: echo-frame-mux with a storm behind it didn't run). `budgets -Against` and `e2e -Smoke` weren't run: `lbterm.ps1` refuses both while the displays sleep (PITFALLS 239).

### Not traced yet

Segment 1, the program to the model:

- Whether Claude Code draws streamed text as it comes from the real API. Against run 5's stand-in (one text block in 444 deltas over 13.9 s) it drew only its spinner and a token count, and the whole answer when the block ended. How: a recording of one of Lachlan's own sessions (a test server of his with LBTERM_PTY_RECORD, a real turn), his call, since it reaches the real API.
- Lachlan's own mix of frames: how many tool calls a working session of his makes a minute and how long it spends with only the spinner, which sets proposal 3's saving at his load (run 5's turn is a scripted 10 calls in 5.1 s). How: his transcripts' tool calls a minute, or the recording above.
- Where the conpty host's 25 to 41 µs a write go, and whether ConPTY 1.25 truly never renders its buffer again (inferred from bytes out equalling bytes in, and from Microsoft's notes). How: `sample.ps1 -ProcessId <a test's OpenConsole>` with -SymPath at Microsoft's symbol server; a test's OpenConsole runs from the build folder, so sample.ps1 doesn't refuse it.
- The split of a parser batch's 140 µs (2.5 recvs, 1 to 3 WSAPolls, the timer, the parse, the model, the spawn) and of a read's 30 to 45 µs (the ReadFile against the loopback send); the rows' splits are estimates. How: a probe build with QPC timers round each part, as perf/key-latency's probe had, or a CPU-sampling ETW trace (WPR with stacks, which needs an elevated prompt Lachlan runs).
- The output pipe's real buffer (CreatePipe with 0), and so how much ConPTY can write before the reader's absence blocks it. How: GetNamedPipeInfo on the pipe's handle (filedescriptor imports it already).
- Run 1's per-program write counts (lost with its run.json); the stand-ins' code and ConPTY's bytes fix them closely, but they weren't read.
- Whether the parser waits on the terminal lock at Lachlan's real load (18 sessions, his window's pushes): no sample of it at 8 tabs, 93% of the main thread's time under a full-speed writer in finding 91. How: run 1 again with -Claude 16 and a writing tab in view.
- What the 3 ms coalescing still buys now that the server's pushes are paced by `lbterm_flow` and `OutputNotice` folds notices: whether frames visibly tear without it. Not for Claude Code, whose frames come whole and bracketed (run 5); for programs that bracket nothing (a shell, other TUIs). How: a try window with `mux_output_parser_coalesce_delay_ms = 0` against such a program, with the frames captured, or its recording through `bench/pty-frames.ps1`.
- How often the batch of a DECSET alone (row 9, PITFALLS 125) has its notice reach the main thread before the frame's batch, so that the server works out a push with nothing in it: a race the code leaves open. How: a count in a probe build of `compute_changes` returning None after a PaneOutput, under claude-output.ps1.

Segment 2, the server's push:

- Lachlan's daily push rate, push size, rows a push and mix of PDUs: his server and window weren't touched. How: his GUI's `metrics` `mux` counters (a PDU type's count and bytes, each way) read over a minute, which is his call; or a test window replaying a real session's output.
- What a push of real Claude Code's frames carries on the wire. By the model (run 5) a frame of its spinner or token count marks 2 rows changed and one that moves its conversation 87 to 89 of 92 (SU over a region of the alternate screen), the answer landing 67; the bytes and the bonus lines on the wire weren't counted. How: claude-output.ps1 with codec=debug and a log of `bonus_lines.len()`.
- The steps under about one sample (4% of the main thread's busy time in run 2): the recv a pane, the send a round, the task hops, the Boxes, `Mux::notify`, the locks. How: sample the main thread alone (`-Tid`, 15,000 samples in 15 s) or an ETW trace of the server's syscalls and context switches.
- How long the main thread waits on terminal locks under Lachlan's load (none caught: the parsers were mostly in recv). How: the same run with flooding tabs (`mux-flood.ps1`) and the main thread sampled alone.
- The alert and title paths (rows 14, 20, 21 and 34, and the GUI's side), not exercised by any run: their encode and their compression a PDU. MUX.md measured their whole. How: `title-storm.ps1` or `status-storm.ps1` with codec=debug and the sampler.
- How many of run 2's 3,097 pushes answered its 82 polls or 82 keys rather than being owed: the log doesn't say. How: log each push's path (owed, key, poll).
- How many bytes the clustered form of the same lines would serialize to, and its zstd time, which decides whether sending `CellStorage::C` is worth it. How: a codec test like `lbterm_codec_cost.rs` serializing a push's lines both ways.
- Why run 2 saw 103 pushes a second where MUX.md's run of the same script saw 238 ("Where the traces disagree").
- What run 2's two samples named `emit_output_for_pane` (the tmux path, which didn't run) really were.

Segment 3, the GUI's half:

- What the client thread does with its 573 µs a PDU: the code accounts for 100 to 150 µs, and the split between the recvs and AFD waits, zstd, varbincode's line building, the merges and the reactor's wakes is unmeasured because run 3's sampler missed. How: run 3 again with `sample.ps1 -ProcessId <the window's pid>` (from `metrics`' pid or the warden's hand-over line) `-Threads 3`, or a timing build round each step of `client_thread_async`; it would also say which thread is the client's ("Where the traces disagree").
- The split of run 3's 0.89 ms a push in the take between the link scan, the LRU puts and drops, the frees and `Mux::notify`; the rows rest on perf/mux-client's timing build (98% the scan) and PERF.md's core-8. A sampled run would show it.
- How many pushes merge, and how many lines a push carries: `lbterm_backlog` has no counter, and `metrics` has only payload sizes. How: a counter on `Merger::add`'s merge arm and on lines a push in `apply_changes_to_surface`; and a flooded pane's cache measured with and without merges, against MUX.md's 2.9 KB a line.
- How many of run 3's 1,786 wakes were the extra turn after a drain (PITFALLS 80). How: a count of waits shorter than about 10 µs, or a histogram of the time waited a wake.
- Why run 3's main build cost 0.21 s of the GUI thread a second where perf/mux-client measured main at 0.59 ("Where the traces disagree").
- Which thread holds async-io's reactor, the client thread or the `async-io` thread, and how often it changes hands. The client thread lets it go after every GUI timer's notify once it has held it 500 µs, and the `async-io` thread takes it by itself after about 20 ms with no tick, after which each socket event costs a hop between the two threads (row 43). CPU time can't tell, since either waits in the same blocking GetQueuedCompletionStatusEx (run 3's `async-io` under 15.6 ms in 10 s, run 4's 47 ms). How: sample both threads' stacks and count which sits in GetQueuedCompletionStatusEx under `react`, or count async-io's `react` calls a thread in a probe build.
- The exact sizes of each async_task allocation, `process_unilateral_inner`'s boxed future and TermWindowNotif's box: estimated, not measured.
- What InvalidateRect costs on this window: run 4's 36 µs a dispatch of the open pane's notice against run 3's 3.65 µs puts it at about 30 µs, since it's the only step the open pane's adds (row 67, "Where the traces disagree"). How: QPC round the call in `lbterm_invalidate_at` in a probe build, under paint-cost.

Segment 4, the frame:

- The GPU's time a frame (the 3.8 MB copy and the pass): no timestamp queries in the build. How: wgpu's timestamp queries in a probe build, or PIX or GPUView.
- What DWM does for each LBTerm present: how much of the pane visual it composes again with no dirty rects, its CPU and GPU a frame for this window, and whether Present1 with dirty or scroll rectangles would cut it. How: `perf/dwm-loops.ps1`'s meter (`Measure-DwmSpan`, DCompositionGetTargetStatistics) on an uncovered window, against a probe build presenting with dirty rects.
- The present to the screen for this flow: present timing was off (paint-cost doesn't set `LBTERM_PRESENT_TIMING`) and the window's screen had other windows coming up. How: `metrics --present on` on an uncovered test window, for `screen_ms` and `after_present_ms`.
- The pace's period in run 4 (the far-right screen's refresh) and the holds under bursts: no frame was held. How: a test printing faster than the period (`perf/mux-flood.ps1`, or paint-cost with -IntervalMs 1) with the state file's `pace`.
- Whether each of paint-cost's pushes carried one line or two: `Start-Sleep -Milliseconds 16` on Windows' 15.6 ms tick likely takes about 31 ms, which would make the 29 frames a second a line each. How: the pane's last line ("writer done after N lines", `lbterm cli get-text`).
- The kernel's cost of the submits and presents (dxgkrnl, the display driver). How: an ETW trace (PresentMon, WPR), which needs elevation.
- What real Claude Code's frames cost the GUI thread. How often they move rows is measured (run 5: 13 of 265 frames, one a tool call; the rest a row in place; What could go, 3), their cost isn't: proposal 3's figures use run 4's cost a row. How: run 5's recording played into a test window's pane at its times (a pane program writing each recorded read), under paint-cost's counters, which also gives proposal 3's before and after on Claude Code's own output.
- The atlas-full path (row 90) on main in a mux window: not run here (no new glyphs in run 4); perf/gpu-memory's glyph bursts (`bench/mem/gpumem.ps1`) measured it on that branch.
- Which row the first scroll lays out besides the line it brings, keyed by content (perf/line-quads-by-content): `perf/line-quads.ps1` counts one line more than the scroll brings on the first scroll of its lone and region modes alike, and none more after (lone: 1, then 0 a scroll; region: the rule the first scroll brings laid out where later rules hit). Not a blank row's cells: `Line::fill_range` prunes trailing blanks, so a row SU makes (`Line::new`) and one ED blanked both hold none, and with bidi off `set_bidi_info` sets no bits. How: a probe build logging each laid-out row's index and shape hash for that frame.

## A window's resize on a mux domain, to every pane at its new size

The window's size changing with its tabs on the mux domain: a drag of its frame (Windows' sizing loop on the GUI thread, from WM_ENTERSIZEMOVE to WM_EXITSIZEMOVE, a size for each move of the pointer), or one size outside a drag (maximized, restored, snapped, moved to a screen of another scale, another program's SetWindowPos). Traced by perf/mux-client on 2026-11-05 from the code, the GUI's `lbterm::resize` debug log (each size, which tabs took it, each pane's size and whether it was sent), metrics' PDU counts and a stack sample of the server's main thread (`perf/sample.ps1`) through `perf/mux-resize.ps1`: twelve tabs on a mux domain of its own, each after 50,000 lines of scrollback, a third of them longer than the window and coloured; a drag through 40 sizes 16 ms apart, then 30 columns narrower in one step and back. Each row is main's step (the installed build, f9f79048), with what perf/mux-client changed after "Since". The costs are that test's, the PC locked.

| # | Step | Where | Process, thread | Runs | Costs | Needs to run | Found out by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGED → `wm_windowposchanged` → `wm_size` → `check_and_call_resize_if_needed` (GetClientRect, compared with the last size) → `WindowEvent::Resized`, `live_resizing` the window's `in_size_move` (set by WM_ENTERSIZEMOVE). Inside the sizing loop `wm_size` also paints and runs every queued task (`SPAWN_QUEUE.run()`). Since: WM_EXITSIZEMOVE says the drag's end even at the drag's last size (`lbterm_size_move_ended`), which said nothing before, when the size changed inside the loop (`lbterm_sized_in_move`), with no paint for it; a move by the title bar says nothing, as before (the review of perf/mux-client found every move painted a frame and dispatched a Resized that upstream didn't) | `window/src/os/windows/window.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each size | WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGED 26 ms a size on main with twelve tabs (1,042 ms over 40), 5.6 to 5.9 since (196 to 236 ms over 40); 24 to 37 ms for a jump, which every tab takes | yes | metrics' messages |
| 2 | `TermWindow::resize`: the window state, `quad_generation`, the swapchain (`webgpu.resize`), then `apply_dimensions` for a drag's size at the same dpi or `scaling_changed`, which ends in it; overlays, the title, the size readout, `window-resized` for Lua | `wezterm-gui/src/termwindow/resize.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each size | in step 1's | yes | the code |
| 3 | `apply_dimensions`: the size in cells, then `Tab::resize` for every tab of the window, in view or not. Since: a drag's size goes to the tab in view alone, and the rest take the size the drag ends at, once, at WM_EXITSIZEMOVE (`lbterm_resize_behind`; `lbterm_extras::resize::Behind`); a size outside a drag still goes to every tab | `resize.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each size | twelve tabs a size: 480 Resize for a drag of 40, 51 since (the tab in view's 40 and the others' 11), 71 counted with the resyncs' (row 11) | each size for the tab in view; for a tab out of view only the drag's last: no, before | the `lbterm::resize` log |
| 4 | `Tab::resize` → the layout key before and after (TabResized in the GUI's own mux when it moved: the window's `update_title_post_status`) → `resize_panes` → `apply_sizes_from_splits` → each pane's `ClientPane::resize`: its dimensions compared, every held line made stale in place (`make_all_stale`), a Resize PDU from a spawned task (two tasks a PDU, `clientpane.rs`'s spawn and the RPC's), the poll brought back (`lbterm_reshaped`). Since the close-pane stage of perf/mux-client: no line is made stale and the poll isn't brought back; the Resize's task asks for the pane's changes once the Resize is answered (`renderable.rs`'s `ask_changes`, row 14), and a size from the server (a resync's, `from_server`) asks at once unless a Resize of the window's own is still out, whose answer will. A zoom (`ClientPane::set_zoomed`) makes nothing stale either: its size comes through here straight after (`TabInner::toggle_zoom`) | `mux/src/tab.rs`, `wezterm-client/src/pane/clientpane.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each size, each tab that takes it | 438 task runs, 4.1 ms, over a drag on main | yes | metrics' tasks |
| 5 | The paint's own check of the window's size (`TermWindow::lbterm_follow_window_size`, in `do_paint_webgpu`): a client rect other than the layout's is laid out again through `resize`. During a drag driven from another thread (a test's SetWindowPos) a paint can see the drag's next size before WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGED reaches the window, and it called `resize` as a size outside a drag, so every tab took that size: 7 of a drag's 40 sizes. Since: it passes on whether a drag is under way (`Behind::dragging`) | `wezterm-gui/src/termwindow/mod.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | when a paint sees a size the window hasn't been told of | twelve tabs resized each time | yes, as the drag's | the `lbterm::resize` log (PITFALLS 139) |
| 6 | The client thread writes each request as it comes off its channel and flushes it alone (`client_thread_async`), so a jump's twelve Resize reach the server as twelve writes | `wezterm-client/src/client.rs` | GUI, client thread | each PDU | | yes; one write for what's queued would do | the code |
| 7 | Server: `SessionHandler::process_one`'s Resize arm. On main, a main-thread task a request, applied in the order they came: `LocalPane::resize` (ResizePseudoConsole under the pty's lock, `lbterm_ptyrec::resized`, `Terminal::resize` under the terminal's lock, row 8), then step 9; every other pane's keys and pushes wait behind it. Since: `lbterm_resize::Resizes::ask` notes the newest size asked for the pane and the request's answer; a pane not being resized is resized on a thread of its own (`lbterm-resize`), one resize at a time a pane, the panes side by side; requests that come meanwhile wait, and the newest is applied after, once; a size the pane has already isn't applied; the main thread rebuilds the tab and answers when it's done (`done`). The pane's other requests that come while it's being resized wait for its resizes and run after them, in the order they came (`Resizes::after`: its input, keys, the mouse, pastes and writes, and the window's poll and its fetches of rows). Taken at once, as this branch first took them, they overtook the resize: the poll and the fetch the window sends straight after its Resize (row 4's `lbterm_reshaped`, row 13) were answered before the resize thread had the terminal, and the window heard of the pane's output twice, so a one-tab window resized 80 px drew six frames where the installed build drew five in 15 of 48 resizes, and in 2 of them the poll's answer carried the pane's old size, which the window took as the pane's until its resync put the server's new one back and fetched the rows again (PITFALLS 145). Since, 1 of 24 drew a sixth frame, with no second output (the installed build 1 of 24, a status light's), and none put a size back. The review found ListPanes still answered at once: a resync during a drag listed panes at sizes their threads hadn't reached, and blocked the main thread on the terminal of each pane a thread was rewrapping (`codec_pane_tree` reads each pane's dimensions, title, folder and cursor under its lock), and an installed window, which sends listed sizes back, never settled (row 11, PITFALLS 159 and 160). Since the fix-up, ListPanes and every request that resizes panes on the main thread (SplitPane, KillPane's prune, SetPaneZoomed, MovePaneToNewTab, AdjustPaneSize) wait until no pane is being resized (`Resizes::after_all`), and GetPaneRenderableDimensions behind its pane's resize; a resize that panics on its thread is answered as an error naming the pane (`guarded`, PITFALLS 161) | `wezterm-mux-server-impl/src/sessionhandler.rs`, `lbterm_resize.rs`, `mux/src/localpane.rs` | server, main (since: `lbterm-resize` threads) | each Resize | on main 34.8 s of the main thread for a drag of 40 with twelve tabs (72 ms a pane), every echo held 35 s and more; 0.76 s for a jump. Since: 112 ms of the main thread for the drag (its CPU 978 ms on the threads), 9.3 to 9.6 ms for a jump (its CPU 359 to 376 ms) | yes, once a pane for each size it ends at; on the main thread: no | a stack sample of the main thread: 100% under `TerminalState::resize` |
| 8 | `TerminalState::resize` → `increment_seqno` → both screens' `Screen::resize`: the trailing blank rows below the cursor pruned, then for the primary screen, when the width changes, `rewrap_lines`: every line drained into a new VecDeque (grown by doubling from empty), every line's sequence number moved on, `last_cell_was_wrapped` on each (a walk of a clustered line's graphemes to its last cell), a wrapped run joined (`Line::append_line`: each cell of the next line made a `Cell` and appended) and a line longer than the width cut (`Line::wrap`: its cells collected into a vector, each written into a new line a grapheme at a time). The alternate screen only truncates or marks its rows. Since: the deque made the lines' size; a clustered line's join and cut a run at a time (`ClusteredLine::lbterm_append_line`, `lbterm_wrap`; printable ASCII cut by bytes with no walk), whether it's wrapped read from its last run (`lbterm_last_wrapped`); a line that keeps its row and its cells keeps its sequence number, one joined, cut or moved to another row gets the new one | `term/src/screen.rs`, `wezterm-surface/src/line/line.rs`, `clusterline.rs` | server, main (since: `lbterm-resize`) | each pane, each size that changes its width | on main about 72 ms a pane: under `Screen::resize`, `Line::wrap` 67% (its collect 28%), `append_line` 25%, `last_cell_was_wrapped` 4%, the allocator about 45% of the samples; since, 25 to 31 ms a pane for 120 to 90 columns (a jump of twelve panes 359 and 376 ms of the server's CPU) | the rows in view and the scrollback's rows: yes; the whole scrollback at each size: doubtful (What could go, 2) | the stack sample; term's and wezterm-surface's tests |
| 9 | `rebuild_splits_sizes_from_contained_panes`: the tab's split sizes from its panes' (each pane's dimensions under its terminal's lock), then TabResized to every connection, whether the layout moved or not. Since: only when the layout key moved (`notify_resized_if_changed`); and since the fix-up once a tab none of whose panes is being resized any more had one resized (`Book::finished`'s `rebuild`), where it ran as each pane's resize ended and so waited on the main thread for a pane of the same tab its thread was still rewrapping. Since the fix-up too, a TabResized from a rebuild whose resizes one connection alone asked for goes to every connection but that one (`rebuilding_as` marks the rebuild, each connection's subscriber in `dispatch.rs` asks `is_own` as the notification reaches it, a connection known by `Conn`, a number `SessionHandler` takes as it's made): the layout is the one that window laid out itself, and a resync it would answer with is row 11 | `mux/src/tab.rs`, `lbterm_resize.rs`, `dispatch.rs` | server, main | each tab, once its panes' resizes are over (before: each resize applied) | 250 TabResized in a drag on main, 183 with the branch's first changes | once a tab's panes are all resized: yes | metrics' PDUs |
| 10 | ConPTY redraws the pane at its new size: output, so the pane is owed a push; `compute_changes` reads every line after a resize (its `ChangeBounds` forgotten), and with every line's sequence number moved on, the push's dirty rows were the whole scrollback (one range of 50,000 rows). Since: only the lines joined, cut or moved | `term/src/lbterm_changes.rs`, `wezterm-mux-server-impl/src/lbterm_push.rs` | server, main | each pane after its resize | the read of 50,000 lines' sequence numbers | the whole scrollback dirty: no | the code; term's `lbterm_rewrap` tests |
| 11 | GUI: each TabResized → `client.rs`'s arm queues a task → `ClientDomain::resync` (one at a time, one more after: `coalesce.rs`) → ListPanes (the server walks every tab and pane) → `process_pane_list` → each tab `sync_with_pane_tree` at the server's sizes → `ClientPane::resize`, which sent each size back as a Resize. During a drag the server's sizes are older than the window's, so the resync sent the older ones back and the server rewrapped every pane to them, said TabResized again and the window resynced again: 100 Resize more than the drag's 480, and a tab could end at a size the window wasn't. Since: the server's sizes are taken without being sent (`pane/lbterm_sizes.rs`, `from_server`), then a tab the GUI had gets the size its window gave it back, sending only what differs from the server's. That put-back sent about 21 Resize more in a drag (the review's run: 35 ListPanes and 72 Resize for 51 sizes), and two windows showing one tab at different sizes would each have sent their own back at every TabResized for ever; since the fix-up it's put back without being sent, and only while a pane of the tab has a Resize the server hasn't answered (`Unanswered`, `tab_unanswered`): the listing was asked before the server had the window's size, which it will reach and say. With none out the server's size is the tab's, as upstream took it. A server of this build lists only once no pane is being resized (row 7), so a listing asked after a Resize has its size, and tells a window nothing of a layout its own Resizes alone made (row 9), so a drag brings it no resync at all. A window of any build before this one resyncs on every TabResized and sends back each listed size that isn't its own, and during a drag its next size has often gone before the listing comes: against this branch's server before the fix-up the installed window's drag never ended (the review: 11,446 Resize in 108 s), with ListPanes held behind the resizes it ended 4 to 20 columns short of the window (1,104 and 1,268 Resize, 74 and 89 ListPanes), and against the installed server it never ended within 90 s either (stage 3) | `wezterm-client/src/client.rs`, `domain.rs`, `pane/lbterm_sizes.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each TabResized | 370 resync task runs, 120 ms, in a drag on main | the resync for the tree: yes; sending the server's own sizes back: no | the `lbterm::resize` log |
| 12 | The push's dirty rows on the GUI: `apply_changes_to_surface` popped each row of each dirty range, and for a row above the viewport `make_stale` popped it again: two lookups a row, 50,000 rows a pane after a resize. Since: rows above the viewport only leave the cache, so the range or the cache is walked, whichever is shorter (`drop_rows_above`) | `wezterm-client/src/pane/renderable.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each push with dirty rows | | the rows the cache holds: yes; every row of the range: no | the code; `lbterm_renderable_test.rs` |
| 13 | The tab in view's frame at the new size, and its lines fetched again (GetLines) as they're drawn: made stale in step 4. Since the close-pane stage: drawn as held, with nothing fetched for a row the window holds (a row it doesn't is fetched, as before); the rows the rewrap changed come in row 14's push, their lines with it, and are drawn in the frame it brings | `render/pane.rs`, `renderable.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each size | one GetLines a resize, and a frame of its own where the push had come first (budgets' close-pane, a frame more than the installed build in 67 of 75 closes) | the frame: yes; fetching rows the window holds: no | metrics; perf/mux-resize-rows.ps1 |
| 14 | Since the close-pane stage: the push the resize asked for. The server's GetPaneRenderChanges arm, held behind the pane's resize on this build's server (`Resizes::after`, row 7) and taken in order on an older one's main thread, so it's worked out once the rewrap is done: it names the rows the rewrap joined, cut or moved and those ConPTY's redraw wrote since the connection's last push (on an older server, whose rewrap moves every line on, the pane's whole scrollback), with the lines of those in view, then the LivenessResponse. In the GUI it goes through the backlog (`lbterm_backlog.rs`) to `apply_changes_to_surface` and `rows_named`, a tab out of view's as the tab in view's, with no frame for one out of view. The row the cursor leaves is no longer fetched unless local echo drew a guess into it (`cursor_moved`; the pane-output flow, row 59) | `wezterm-client/src/pane/clientpane.rs`, `renderable.rs` (`ask_changes`, `rows_named`, `cursor_moved`), `wezterm-mux-server-impl/src/sessionhandler.rs` | server, main; GUI, client thread and GUI thread | once a Resize is answered, and once a size from the server | a GetPaneRenderChanges round and one `compute_changes`, which finds nothing new when ConPTY's redraw pushed the pane already | yes: a quiet pane's ConPTY may write nothing after a resize, and a poll comes only with a frame that draws the pane, so without it a tab out of view keeps its old rows until it next prints: the installed build's tabs out of view still held their rows from before the rewrap 1 s after a resize (3 of 3, in each part of perf/mux-resize-rows.ps1 that looks) and drew them in the first frame that showed them, then fetched them | perf/mux-resize-rows.ps1; the GUI's `wezterm_client::pane::renderable` trace |

What a drag and a jump came to, measured with `perf/mux-resize.ps1` (each once on the installed build, f9f79048, and on this branch's build at each step of the work, 2026-11-05, the PC locked; MUX.md, "A window's resize", has the final numbers against the installed build's): on main a drag of 40 sizes left the server's main thread busy 35 s (34.8 s of its CPU), a key typed straight after it unechoed in 30 s and the panes unsettled; the branch's drag echoes the key in 62 to 70 ms and has every pane at its size in 186 to 213 ms. A jump of 30 columns held every pane's echo 770 ms on the branch before the resizes left the main thread, 143 ms after. After the review's fix-up (rows 7, 9 and 11; 2026-11-05 22:25 to 22:28) a drag of this build's window brings no ListPanes and no TabResized (40 Resize for 40 sizes: the review had 35 ListPanes and 72 Resize), its key echoed in 60 ms and every pane at its size in 182 ms; and the installed window on this server, which sends every size for every tab, settles too, in 232 and 276 ms with 480 and 479 Resize, where it had never settled (MUX.md, "A window's resize", has every pairing).

What could go, from these rows (not built):

1. A local pane's resize still rewraps on the GUI thread, every tab of the window for a size outside a drag, one after another (the same 25 to 70 ms a pane of 50,000 lines): the same thread a pane as the server's would take it off the GUI thread.
2. Every size rewraps a pane's whole scrollback, though a size passed through is never seen above the screen: rewrapping the rows in view at once and the rest when they're read or the size has held would make a pane's resize cost its screen. Its stable rows above would move later, which every reader of a stable row (the scroll strip's marks, selection, search, a mux client's cache) would have to take.
3. A resync for every TabResized: ListPanes lists every tab and pane, the server walking each pane's process for its title and folder, for the one tab that moved. A TabResized that carried the tab's tree would do.
4. The client thread flushes each request alone (row 6).
5. Built (the close-pane stage of perf/mux-client, 2026-11-07; rows 4, 13 and 14): the window takes the push's word, and asks for the push. Before, a pane's resize made every row the window holds stale (`ClientPane::resize`'s `make_all_stale`), so the frame after it fetched the rows in view again (one GetLines a resize, on main and since), though the push after the server's rewrap says which rows changed (PITFALLS 142). Where the server had the size already, a pane closed beside the one that grows (the server prunes the closed pane and resizes the other itself before the window's Resize comes), the installed build's server resized it again for nothing and pushed every row, so its window fetched none; this branch's skips that resize (`is_size`), so its window fetches the rows it made stale and draws them in a frame of their own: budgets' close-pane 3 frames to 4, with one GetLines more and one TabResized and one ListPanes fewer (a split's pane closed ten times on each build, 2026-11-05: a fourth frame in 12 of 20 closes, each with the Resize, against none on the installed build). Taking the push's word for which rows a resize changed saves the fetch on every resize; a tab out of view, whose pushes the window takes later, was looked at first, and the push wasn't sure to come: the server answers a resize with no push, a quiet pane's ConPTY may write nothing, and the poll a resize brought back (`PollPace::reshaped`) goes out only with a frame that draws the pane, once 20 ms have passed since the last. So the resize asks for it (row 14), of a server of any build, and a tab out of view holds the server's rows before it's shown, where the installed build's held its old ones and drew them in the first frame that showed it (perf/mux-resize-rows.ps1, which checks it; MUX.md, "The rows a resize changed", has the runs). Measured with the budget runner against the installed build (2026-11-07): close-pane 3 frames on both, its GetLines 2 to 0 over the runs, and 80 to 0 in split, zoom and resize each; its messages and split's still higher (7 to 9, 11 to 13), untraced.
6. A push owed to a pane being resized (output it printed before its resize began, its notice still owed in the connection's round, `lbterm_flow.rs`) is worked out on the main thread, which then waits for the pane's terminal lock that its `lbterm-resize` thread holds through the rewrap (25 to 30 ms a pane of 50,000 lines), and every other pane's keys and pushes wait with it. Output the pane prints during the rewrap waits in its parser for the same lock (pane-output flow, row 12), so it brings no notice until the rewrap ends. A round that skipped a pane being resized and came back to it once its resizes were over (`Resizes::after`) would keep the main thread free. Not measured apart: mux-resize.ps1's server main thread over a drag (67 to 151 ms since the fix-up) holds it with everything else.
7. The server's own prune of a pane whose program ended (`Mux::prune_dead_windows`, then the tab's `prune_dead_panes` → `remove_pane_if`, `mux/src/tab.rs`, which resizes the pane or split left with `resize`) resizes the pane left beside it on the main thread, outside the book, so it could run beside that pane's resize thread and leave its pseudoconsole and its terminal at different sizes, which the requests held behind every resize (row 7) no longer can. Rare: a program ending while its tab is resized.
8. A thread is made for each resize (`std::thread::Builder::spawn` in `Resizes::start`), whatever the pane's scrollback, and ends with it: a pane of a few hundred lines rewraps in well under a millisecond, and the thread's start and end cost the server about as much again (budgets' font-zoom, two Resizes a run: its threads gone by the end 0.53 ms of CPU a run, the main thread 0.13 ms less). One kept thread a pane while resizes come, or a few for the server, would keep the main thread free without making one each time. Not measured apart.
9. Built in part (the fix-up of 2026-11-08): only the last Resize answered asks (`lbterm_sizes::Sent::answered`), so a drag whose answers come behind its next sizes asks once, at its end; a pane in view is polled with its frames meanwhile, as each Resize sent brings its poll pace back (`update_last_send`). Measured in MUX.md's 'The rows a resize changed'. What follows is what it was: the ask after each Resize answered went out whether ConPTY's redraw had pushed the pane already or not: a GetPaneRenderChanges round a Resize, about one a size for the tab in view in a drag (perf/mux-resize-rows.ps1: 18 or 19 in a drag of 12 sizes, three tabs out of view taking its last). This build's server could owe every connection the pane once its resize is done (`lbterm_flow.rs`'s Pending), which would make the ask unneeded against it, but an older server doesn't, and the window can't tell them apart. Not measured apart.
10. Since the close-pane stage a jump's push for each tab out of view comes as the jump settles (row 14), where on a quiet pane it came only once the tab was shown, and after a narrower rewrap of 50,000 lines it names most of the scrollback as moved, which the window walks its cache for (row 12's `drop_rows_above`, the cache or the range, whichever is shorter): perf/mux-resize.ps1's jump of twelve tabs cost the GUI thread 375 ms over 0.48 s, its longest wait for a message 151.5 ms (2026-11-07, one run; the build before the stage not run beside it). The pane-output flow's proposal 2 (What could go 2: a hidden tab's output stops at the server) would hold those pushes until a tab is shown, and the walk with them. A push that named the rows a rewrap moved as a shift of the rows below a point, not each row, would let the window move its cache's keys or drop them in one go.

## A window's start, from the process to its first frame

A new LBTerm window from nothing, on a local domain: the process created, its config run, its first tab, its window and GPU state, its first paint. Measured by perf/fonts-core with `metrics`' `start` marks (five rounds of each variant of one build, interleaved, 2026-11-01), so each cost below is a range over those rounds; PERF.md, "A window's start, step by step", has the runs, and "What font setup costs" the fonts' part. Times from the process's creation unless a row says a length.

| # | Step | Where | Process, thread | Runs | Costs | Needs to run | Found out by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The process created, to `main` | Windows, the loader | GUI, main | each start | about 30 ms (an 83 MB executable) | yes: nothing LBTerm can change | `start` marks |
| 2 | The config read and run | `config`, Lachlan's `lbterm.lua` | GUI, main | each start | ends at 62 to 68 ms, the Lua about 43 of it | yes | `start` marks |
| 3 | The GPU warm-up started: the process's wgpu instance and adapter, on a thread of its own | `termwindow/gpu_warm.rs`, `warm_up()` | GUI, the warm-up thread | once a process | 308 to 524 ms, nearly all in choosing the adapter: wgpu makes a D3D12 device on every adapter DXGI lists to ask what it can do | doubtful: one device on the adapter LBTerm will use would do (PERF.md's first "what LBTerm can still change") | `start` marks |
| 4 | The first tab: its ConPTY and its program | `mux`, `localpane.rs` | GUI, main | each start | ready at 108 to 251 ms, ConPTY and pwsh about 45 | yes | `start` marks |
| 5 | The window's fonts | `wezterm-font`; `wezterm-font/src/lbterm.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each window | under 0.1 ms (the bundled fonts parsed once a process, the GDI lookups that copied 11 to 14 font files gone) | yes | `start` marks, the font log |
| 6 | The operating system's window | `window/src/os/windows/window.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each window | 57 to 64 ms | yes | `start` marks |
| 7 | The window's GPU state: it waits for the warm-up's adapter, then makes its device, swapchain and pipeline | `termwindow/webgpu.rs`, `new_impl()` | GUI, GUI thread | each window | from 175 to 335 ms; the device 64 to 117 ms, the swapchain 4 to 8, the pipeline 26 to 74 | the wait and the device and pipeline per window doubtful: a later window could take the first's (needs `gpu_lost.rs` to rebuild every window on one loss) | `start` marks |
| 8 | The render state | `renderstate.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each window | 9 to 25 ms | yes | `start` marks |
| 9 | The first paint: the pass, then the draw, which makes the compositor's 27 surfaces | `render/paint.rs`; `render/composited.rs`; `termwindow/compositor.rs` | GUI, GUI thread | each window | begins at 542 to 804 ms and takes 170 to 270: the pass 35 to 56, the draw 118 to 198, of which the 27 surfaces 126 to 169 (3 to 15 ms each, whatever its size, and every one made again when the window's size changes) | the surfaces doubtful: PERF.md's finding 78 | `start` marks, compositor counters |
| 10 | The state file's first frame (a test window's) | `termwindow/state.rs` | GUI, the state writer | each test window | 746 to 1,108 ms after the process's creation | only in a test window | `start` marks |

On a mux domain a step comes before 4: the GUI starts the server only after ten tries at its missing socket over 2.25 s, the whole of `start`'s `ready_ms` there (2,748 to 2,835 ms): needs to run, no (PERF.md, finding 25). Not traced yet: the server's own start, and what wgpu and the driver do inside steps 3 and 7.

## A key, to its echo on the screen

Not traced as rows yet. CODEMAP.md's "A key press" has the path, and perf/key-latency measured it (PERF.md, "A key's echo, after perf/key-latency", 2026-10-31), so its audit starts from this:

- One key shows in the first frame after it, 8.9 to 10.4 ms from the key (`key-echo`, a mux domain and local), where it took two frames and 21 ms or more before: a key no longer asks for a frame of its own unless that frame draws something new, and on a mux domain the server's answer to a key asks for none either (findings 35 and 68).
- The frame starts painting 4.6 to 4.7 ms after the key locally and 5.7 ms on a mux domain (`present`'s `paint_ms`); its pass, draw and presents take about 4.5 ms more; then DWM's next composition.
- On the echo's way back: the program's echo through ConPTY, and the mux parser's coalesce wait of 3 ms (`mux_output_parser_coalesce_delay_ms`; at 0, 6.2 ms locally), which ends when it's due (finding 67, `lbterm_wait.rs`).
- The mux server spent 14.9 to 15.8 ms a key walking the PC's processes for the pane's title before perf/key-latency, 1.7 to 2.1 ms after: a pane's processes are walked only when its job says one started or ended in a way that changes what it shows (finding 49; CODEMAP.md, "Files and processes, off the GUI thread").
- Keys faster than a refresh share frames paced to the screen: on the 60 Hz test screen each key falls about 1.7 ms further behind (16.7 ms frames against 15 ms keys); on Lachlan's 160 Hz screen they don't fall behind.
- Under load (a writer at full speed beside the prompt) the echo waits behind the writer's frames and the paint behind the writer's hold on the terminal (finding 44): 27.1 to 30.1 ms on a mux domain, 50 to 67 locally.
- Into the program: the server's (or a local pane's) `key_down` encodes the key under the pane's terminal lock and hands it to the terminal's writer thread (term's `ThreadedWriter`, one a pane), which writes it to the pty's pipe; a binding's text (a pane's `writer()`) goes through the same thread since perf/mux-client, which found it written to the pipe on the caller's thread, so that it could reach the program ahead of a key typed before it (PITFALLS 137).
- A write that doesn't get there (perf/mux-client, from the code, 2026-11-07; MUX.md, "A write to a pane"). On a mux pane a write is queued as every request is (`send_pdu`, upstream's, since the fix-up of 2026-11-08 took out the failed-writes stage's lock round it): while the connection is down it waits in the client's channel with the keys round it, in its order (PITFALLS 172 and 178). The client thread sets the link's state (`lbterm_link::Link`, an atomic) as a connection ends and once a reconnect succeeds, and only the scroll strip's seek reads it, once a burst (`ClientPane::lbterm_link_down`). The write's task, on the GUI thread, awaits the answer as before and works out from it whether it got there (`Failure::of`: a match on the answer's PDU, nothing more). Only a failure costs more: a log line, `mux::lbterm_writes::failed`, one hop to the GUI thread (`spawn_into_main_thread`) to find the window holding the pane (`Mux::resolve_pane_id`, a lock on the mux's windows) and one `Window::notify` there (a task hop, PITFALLS 81), the strip's note set, and one frame to draw it (the card's, and its wake 4 s on when it goes). The note is the pane's tab's (`Note::tab`, from that `resolve_pane_id`), and the strip asks the mux for its window's active tab only while such a note is held (`lbterm_scroll_shown_note`), which a frame with no note never does. When the client gives up reconnecting, its thread fails what still waits as never sent (`lbterm_link::GaveUp`) as it ends: one `try_send` a request.
- The echo's frame lays out again the rows whose line quad cache key the key changed: the cursor's row (its text and the cursor on it) and the row the cursor left, each a full layout (row 87 of the pane output flow, `render_screen_line`, tens of µs). Since feat/hex-swatch each such layout also walks the row's cells once for `#RRGGBB` codes (`render/hex.rs`, about a hundred cells, the last cell's wrapped bit taken in the same walk; HEX.md, "Where a code is found"), and draws nothing more unless the row holds one.
- The swapchains add nothing at rest: DirectComposition ones, FLIP_DISCARD with three buffers, presented Fifo with a frame latency of 2, and nothing is queued for a key at rest, so the acquire doesn't wait and the present goes up at DWM's next composition.

Not known yet: each step's own cost between the key's arrival and the frame's start, and what the GUI thread does with the 4.6 ms.

## A menu, from what opens it to the end of its fade

Every menu (the tab menu, the prompt list, the Conductor's menu, the InMail list, the agenda, the context pill's list, the + menu) goes through the same steps since 2026-11-05: render/menu_fade.rs on the GUI's side, `lbterm_motion::menu` for what each number does, `Compositor::prepare_menus` and `acquire_menus` for the tree. All on the GUI thread but the motion, which DWM plays. Traced from the code; no step's cost measured on its own. A window at rest with the InMail list open (`budgets -Only inbox-list`, 5 rounds against main's f9f790489, the PC locked, 2026-11-05) costs what it did with the list in `top`: the GUI's CPU 1.53 and 1.49 ms/s, the server's 0.059 and 0.061, private memory 385 and 386 MB, handles 849 and 856, none told apart at 99% (the handles higher in 5 of 5 pairs, by 6.2: the slot's group, its two surfaces and the highlight's clip).

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The click or key that opens it sets the menu's state (`Open`, the list's `list`) and invalidates the window | each menu's file (render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_open_tab_menu`, render/new_tab.rs `lbterm_new_tab_open_plus`, ...) | once per open | small: the tab menu measures its widest row once here (`lbterm_menu_text`) | yes |
| 2 | The paint pass marks every slot undrawn | render/paint.rs `paint_pass` → `lbterm_menu_fade_begin` | every paint | four slots' fields cleared | yes: a slot not drawn in this paint is the sign its menu closed |
| 3 | Each open menu draws its light, ring and fill (`lbterm_menu_card`), its rows and words (the tab menu's keys among them, one word each), and hands over its lit row (`lbterm_menu_light`) instead of drawing it | each menu's `paint_lbterm_*`, render/menu_fade.rs | every paint while open | the quads; words laid out only when they change (render/words.rs). The light is one falloff since 2026-11-05 (`lbterm_menu_glow`): 10 bands, each four sides and four fans of 12 round the corners, about 520 quads where the four rings were 28, and the bands' colours (`light_bands`: 11 knots, each `menu_glow_alpha`'s 50-step bisection twice, about 1,100 `powf`s). Made only when the card's place or size, the background under it or the window's own key change (`lbterm_menu_glow_key`), and kept in the slot's glow z-layer otherwise (`lbterm_kept_layer`, render/kept.rs, as the panel's light is: `menu-glow-tab`, `-prompt`, `-conductor`, `-list`, and the picker's `picker-glow`, in the state file's `visuals.built` on the frame that makes it). It was made every paint the menu was in until the fix-up, under full-speed output in the pane below too; a paint of an open menu now only checks the key | yes |

The highlight on the Colour row (traced 2026-11-07, the unlocked captures' finding: a second Down 400 ms after the first, at slowdown 5, "didn't visibly move the highlight"): the tab menu hands its highlight a row only for a row (render/tab_menu.rs, `paint_lbterm_tab_menu`'s `lit`, false for `Item::Colour`); a swatch with the keys is ringed on the card instead, at once (the PROJECT ring, white's 0.9, round the swatch). So Down from Rename onto the Colour row lights its first swatch, "no colour", with that ring, and the highlight's light goes out where it is on `hover` (`menu::light` with no row: 250 ms, 1.25 s at slowdown 5); it doesn't move, and that's what the capture saw. Down again from the swatches to the next row brings the light back up there, its edges gliding from where they were, as a Down during any glide does: `light` takes each edge from where it is with its speed (`the_highlight_glides_between_rows_on_hover_from_wherever_it_is`). Not a fault, but Lachlan's 12 of 2026-11-08 asked for it: since then the Colour row lights as every row does when the keys or the pointer are on one of its swatches (`paint_lbterm_tab_menu`'s `lit` asks `menu::same_row`, which puts every swatch on the Colour row), so Down from Rename glides the highlight there on `hover` (`menu.tab.glide`) rather than putting its light out, the swatch's ring still saying which swatch has the keys, and Down again glides it on. Without the compositor, the row drawn lit on the card, the rings round the swatch with the keys and the tab's own, and the inside of "no colour", are cut out of the lit row's colour rather than the card's (`swatch_under`). The pointer on the row's label, off the swatches, lights nothing, as the row does nothing there (`Layout::hit`). menu-keys.ps1's step 3 holds it: on the build before (2026-11-08) Down from Rename played `menu.tab.light` (the light going out) and no glide, and on the stage's build the glide alone.

Seen on screen (2026-11-07, 22:00 to 22:05, the PC unlocked, against slot-5's build at the merge 520e4d4ea; `motion/capture.ps1` at slowdown 5 unless said, frames in slot-5's `target/motion/unlocked-*` and `conductor-spinner`, each looked at frame by frame):

- **The hairline's pulse** (step 3's `menu_lamp`, a key no row takes; `unlocked-hairline` and, at real speed, `unlocked-hairline-1x`; the tab menu opened by `-Setup` and settled 1.5 s before Q was posted). The hairline is one pixel at this window's 96 DPI, the row just above the card's top (y 95 for a card at y 96), the panel's red over the window: RGB 255, 138, 92 at rest, luma 159.6. Q lifts it to 255, 179, 149 (luma 193.0, +33.4, 21% brighter): 35% of the way to white, the lamp's peak, exactly. At real speed it was up within 60 ms of the key's frame, held its peak about 55 ms (frames at 88 and 113 ms), back under a tenth of its rise by 217 ms and at rest by 315 ms: one pixel's line about a fifth brighter for about 150 ms. At slowdown 5 the same shape over about 1.1 s (above a tenth of its rise to 796 ms, at rest by about 1.2 s). Whether that reads at real speed is Lachlan's call (his rule: decoration he asked for must read at real speed; NEW-TAB.md, section 6) (it didn't: his 9a of 2026-11-08, the next bullet).
- **The key's flare** (Lachlan's 9a of 2026-11-08, which replaced the pulse above; the key flow's step 7, `menu_lamp`): captured at real speed and at slowdown 5 on 2026-11-08 at 16:02, the flare's build (4ddee8473) and the build before it (c3d895113, the pulse above) in turn in one warden job with the measuring lock (`bump-before-1x`, `bump-after-1x`, `bump-before-5x`, `bump-after-5x` in slot-5's target/motion, each with `flare-card.png` and `flare-corner.png`, the frames nearest 0 to 500 ms after the key, and `flare.gif`, the card at real speed or slowed as captured), the tab menu opened by `-Setup 'post,rclick,row,0'` and settled 1.5 s before Q was posted; the PC unlocked, the right-most screen covered by the DashboardsBox (bun) and Discord, 98% each, which PrintWindow draws through. Read out from the card's right side at its middle (x 298, over the pane's blank rows; times from the key's post; lbterm/motion/menu-edge.py, the sheets by timesheet.py and the GIFs by gif.py): the hairline went from the panel's red, RGB 255, 138, 92 (luma 159.6), to white, 255, 255, 255 (+95.4, 60% brighter), at 95 ms, above half its lift from 41 to 209 ms (168) and above a tenth to 347 ms (306), where the pulse before it went to 255, 179, 149 (+33.4) and was above half for 108 ms and a tenth for 193. The light round the card, which didn't move before, lifted 71.0 levels of luma 2 px out (140, 38, 32 to 211, 109, 103, 120% brighter), 39.0 4 px out, 23.0 6 px out and 5.0 9 px out, at the same times: a pink-white band round the whole card, corners included, its falloff kept. The card's fill 4 px in didn't move (0.0). At slowdown 5 the same shape over five times the span (the hairline above half 852 ms, the pulse before it 635). The picker beside the rail is a faded card of the other kind (`StripCard`, CODEMAP.md, "Where each feature lives"): it draws its last layout again each frame while it fades and wakes a paint for the end; the menus do neither.

Opening, measured whole (2026-11-05, stage 6; PERF.md, "The menus' fade, by `budgets -Against`"): the tab menu, the InMail list, the prompt list and the Conductor's view each show in the same frame as on main (`shown_frame` 1, or 2 for the prompts after the click's), in the same time to within a millisecond, and settle about 250 ms later as their fade ends. With the state file on, a window paints one frame more 20 ms after the fade (termwindow/state.rs, `lbterm_state_frame`, so its snapshot says `animating: false`): step 8's "nothing wakes" holds for Lachlan's window, not a test window, so a budget's `frames` there counts one more for every menu that fades, and that frame costs what a paint of the open menu costs: about a millisecond for the tab menu, about 10 ms for the prompt list, which lays its 40 rows out and shapes them on every paint it's in (render/prompt.rs; PERF.md, "What the cards cost"). The fix-up's recheck (PERF.md, same section) found one more thing in the tab menu's opening: 20 of 75 runs on the branch's build spent 40 to 172 ms of the GUI thread in the same 2 frames and 4 messages (main's never past 6 ms). Traced on 2026-11-07 (stage 2 of landing 2's follow-up), below: it's the measuring's own, a test window's timer spinning, and main's code, fixed on this branch on 2026-11-07 (step 6 there).

### Opening, measured whole

One opening of the tab menu in a test window (`budgets`' `tab-menu-opening`: twelve tabs on a mux domain, a right click on the first pad with the pointer resting on it), step by step, from the per-run metrics the budget runner keeps (`target/budgets/<stamp>/<scenario>/<round>-<build>/measure.json`, each sample's `top.messages`, `top.tasks` and `spent_ms`, read over the control pipe's `metrics` with a reset before each run; the span runs from just before the click to 300 ms after the settled frame). The numbers are the medians of the fix-up's recheck (2026-11-05 20:10 to 20:20, the PC locked), the branch's 50 runs without the spin (step 6) against main's 75 (f9f790489). All on the GUI thread but the state file's writing.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost, branch (main) | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The press: WM_RBUTTONDOWN, the rail's mouse handling, nothing opens yet | window.rs `wnd_proc`, render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_mouse` | per click | 0.05 ms (0.05) | yes |
| 2 | The release opens the menu: its facts, rows and words (the tab menu's flow below, steps 1, 2, 6 and 7) | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_open_tab_menu` | per click | WM_RBUTTONUP's own time 0.19 ms (0.02: main's menu read only the link rows) | yes |
| 3 | The frame that shows it: the paint pass with the card and its light (the menu's steps 2 to 6 above), the fade started | render/paint.rs, compositor.rs | once | the two paints of steps 3 and 4 2.13 ms together (`gui.paint.impl`), WM_PAINT's own 0.66 ms for both; main's one paint, the menu drawn into `top`, 1.76 and 0.39 | yes |
| 4 | Test windows only: the state file's frame 20 ms after the fade ends (termwindow/state.rs `lbterm_state_wake`, the task at state.rs:192 and its timer at :193), a whole paint of the window with the menu open so its snapshot says `animating: false`; each frame's snapshot is published (`state_probe::publish`) and written by the `lbterm-state` thread | termwindow/state.rs | once per fade | half of step 3's 2.13 ms, about 1 ms; the `lbterm-state` thread 3.83 ms (1.78), off the GUI thread, for two snapshots where main's had one | not for Lachlan's window, which has no state file and never paints it; for the tests it's how `settled` is seen (What could go) |
| 5 | Test windows only: the timing to the screen (present.rs, on with `LBTERM_PRESENT_TIMING` or a `metrics` request with `present` on): each frame hands over the swapchains it presented, each present is watched until DXGI's frame statistics put it on screen, looked at every 20 ms (`Present::arm`'s `now + 20 ms`), and the fade, as motion, opens an episode (`Present::motion`) that's walked over DirectComposition's compositor clock (`walk`, `Clock::sample`: `DCompositionGetStatistics` and `DCompositionGetTargetStatistics` for each compositor frame since the last walk) once it settles | wezterm-gui/src/present.rs `arm`, `wake`, `poll`, `walk_all` | per frame and each timer | the timer's task (present.rs:657, :654 since the fix of step 6) 32 runs, 0.18 ms (30 runs, 0.59: main's menu didn't fade, so its runs watched presents only) | for the budget's times to the screen only; never in Lachlan's window, where it's off |
| 6 | Test windows only: **the episode's looks after its settle.** `Present::arm` arms one timer for the soonest of a watched present's next look and, for each episode playing, `Episode::next_look` (lbterm-extras/src/present.rs): its settle + 30 ms (`SETTLE_LOOK_MS`) while that's to come; past it, with the walk not done, a refresh on (`Episode::refresh`, the screen's vblank as the compositor gives it), the soonest DWM can complete another frame, and the grace's end (`SETTLE_GRACE_MS`, 200 ms after the settle) at the latest; never at or before now. `wake` polls, walks (`walk_all`) and arms again. An episode is finished (`Episode::finished`) once its walk has stepped a completed compositor frame begun after its settle (`Walk::step` sets `done`; `latest()` is the last `FRAME_COMPLETED` frame), or at the grace's end whatever. When DWM completes that frame within the first 30 ms, as it does while anything on the PC is changing, that's one look; when it doesn't (a locked PC, whose lock screen DWM rarely composes), a look a refresh until it does or the grace ends: 11 at 60 Hz (`an_episode_whose_last_frame_never_comes_is_looked_at_a_refresh_apart_to_its_grace`). **Until 2026-11-07 (stage 3 of landing 2's follow-up) this was the spin:** `arm` armed the settle + 30 ms itself, `soonest(settles.min(playing.walked + WALK_EVERY))`, a time already past once it had gone, so the timer's `after` was 0, its task (`spawn_into_main_thread`, `Timer::after(0)`) ran at once, `wake` found the episode not done and `arm` armed the same past time again: a loop through the GUI's spawn queue, about 3.5 µs a turn, until DWM completed a frame or the grace ended, at most 170 ms, and a paint asked meanwhile waited behind its tasks (the state file's frame, step 4, came up to 170 ms late: round 1's run 5 settled at 427 ms against 253) (PITFALLS 164) | present.rs `arm`, `wake`, `walk_all`; lbterm-extras present.rs `Episode::next_look`, `finished` | per episode: one look, or one a refresh to its grace | a look is a poll and a walk over the compositor's frames since the last; before the fix, 25 of the branch's 75 runs ran the task 1,784 to 57,686 times for 13 to 148 ms (round 1's run 5: 42,349 runs, 147 ms of its 167 ms GUI thread, `executor.spawn_delay` 85 ms), none of main's, which had no motion in this span | the looks, for the budget's motion figures; the spin, no: nothing changed between its turns but the clock |

Without the spin a run's GUI thread is 3.22 ms on the branch against 3.54 on main, the process 4.23 against 5.32. The spin is all of the slow population: every one of the 25 runs over 8 ms of GUI thread has the task run over 1,700 times, and none of the 50 under has it over 160. It's in main's present.rs (the branch doesn't touch it) and shows wherever an action plays motion in a test window and DWM then composes nothing for 30 ms: on a locked PC, whose lock screen rarely changes, most of all. Every slow run was measured locked: the fix-up's recheck, and stage 6's 49.9 ms round. Unlocked, with Lachlan at the PC and his windows changing (2026-11-07 18:59, the branch's merged build c4650c329, 51 runs, others taking 40 to 74% of the job's 4 CPUs), not one: the GUI thread 4.7 to 7.5 ms, two runs at 15.6 with 4 frames in place of 2. A scan of every round the budget runner has kept (`target/budgets` in the main checkout and the six slots: 1,212 rounds' `measure.json` with present timing) found 193 with a run over 1,000 turns of the task, main's build among them: on f9f790489 (2026-11-05, locked) `tab-by-key` spun in 11 to 14 of each round's 16 runs at 144 to 151 ms, `zoom` in 13 and 15 of 16 in two rounds, `throughput` in 2 to 7 of 7. So a GUI-thread or CPU budget set from locked rounds of an action with motion may be holding the spin (PITFALLS 164). After the fix (624c78f6f, `budgets -Against` main's build at the merge base, 2026-11-07 19:47 to 19:59, unlocked; PERF.md, "The menus' fade, by `budgets -Against`", Fixed): the tab menu's opening 3.70 ms of GUI thread against main's 3.75, same (+-0.56), every one of the branch's 90 runs 2.54 to 5.54 ms against main's 3.06 to 8.10, the timer's task at most 4 turns a run; the run was unlocked, so it shows the openings match main's, and the lbterm-extras tests show the spin can't come back. At the branch's last code against the context pill's main (1b8b60db against e8edad3f, 2026-11-07 23:05 to 23:39, unlocked; PERF.md, same section, "The branch as it lands"): 3.49 ms of GUI thread against 2.32, higher (+-0.605), and the medians say where it goes: step 3 and 4's two paints 2.09 ms against main's one at 1.63, WM_PAINT 0.77 against 0.45, step 2's release 0.21 against 0.02, so step 4's frame (test windows only) and step 2's facts, which his window pays (0.19 ms a right click), with nothing else; the InMail list, the picker and the rail the same as main's in every metric the rounds measured but the lists' fade (`missed` and the screen's times went unmeasured in 48 of its 50 rounds, the test window covered: PITFALLS 182).

The prompt list's opening (`prompts-opening`, 24.1 ms of GUI thread against main's 10.2) isn't the same cause, though the spin is in its tail too (24 of the branch's 75 runs over 300 turns, up to 156 ms; none of main's). Its runs without the spin take 19.4 ms against 10.2: four paints (the click's frame, the list's, the state file's frame after the fade, and one more) for 21.5 ms of paint (`gui.paint.impl`) against main's three for 8.1, so one more paint of the open list and dearer paints of it, the list laying out and shaping its 40 rows on every paint it's in (render/prompt.rs). The metrics don't split that 13 ms between the paints; a sample at a rate the GUI thread survives would (PITFALLS 165: one at 1,000 a second stalled the window for seconds, and its names put a third of the paint in `lbterm_marks_answered`, a name PITFALLS 96 says not to trust).

Traced paint by paint on 2026-11-08 (the review of the follow-up found the attribution above wrong: main's prompt.rs lays the 40 rows out and shapes them on every paint too, so that can't be what tells the builds apart). The 23:05 rounds' `measure.json`, grouped by each run's paints and who asked for its frames: main's 73 runs of 77 have 3 WM_PAINT and 2 frames (asked by prompt.rs's open and its prompts arriving), paint 8.12 ms and `shape.harfbuzz` 2.91 in all (one paint of the 40 rows, the open's paint having only the "Reading" row); the branch's 72 of 78 have 4 WM_PAINT and 3 frames, the third asked by termwindow/state.rs:200 (the state file's frame), paint 17.76 and harfbuzz 8.51, about three shapings of the list where main makes one; its longest paint during the fade (`motion_paint_ms`) 9.03, the list's paint landing inside the fade, as main's lands outside any motion (main's 0 there is no motion, not a cheaper paint). Then `budgets.ps1 -Scenario prompts-opening -Runs 4 -SlowEvery` on each build (2026-11-08 02:24, the branch's 615330e01 against a copy of the installed main, e8edad3f), every paint part over 1 ms in the window's log: on the branch every run's paint of the list (5.9 to 16.3 ms of pass, the logging's own cost in it) is followed 228 to 242 ms later by a second paint of the open list, the state file's frame (4.0 to 11.4 ms of pass); on main no run has the second. The branch's first paint (the card with its "Reading" row, its light made, `menu-glow-prompt`) logged 2.5 to 2.9 ms of pass, main's mostly under the log's 1 ms. So, of the 8.9 ms more: one more whole paint of the open list, the state file's, which only a test window paints (step 4), the most of it; a dearer first paint, the card drawn into its slot and its light made once (Lachlan's window pays that, once an opening, a millisecond or two); and about one shaping of the list (about 3 ms of harfbuzz) that neither the metrics, a sum over the run, nor the slow log, which sees only parts over 1 ms and its whole pass, puts in a paint. Not ruled out that his window pays that last one: a `shape.harfbuzz` span counted per paint in `metrics` would say. Paints while the list stays open cost what main's do (the slow log's list paints 5.9 to 16.3 ms on the branch, 4.0 to 14.7 on main): the per-paint layout and shaping is main's, and keeping its words (What could go) is a redesign for both.

What could go, or is open (not decided; for Lachlan):

- Step 6, the spin: done (2026-11-07, stage 3 of landing 2's follow-up). `Present::arm` never arms a time already past: past its settle + 30 ms an episode whose walk isn't done is looked at a refresh on, to its grace's end at the latest (`Episode::next_look`, held by two lbterm-extras tests), at most a dozen looks over the 170 ms in place of up to 58,000 turns, and no paint held behind them. It was main's code and every motion budget's, so the budgets set from locked rounds of actions with motion (`tab-by-key`, `zoom`, the throughputs, and the others among the 193 rounds) want measuring again once this is on main, since some hold the spin. `tab-menu-opening`'s budgets (10 and 20 ms) are what the runs without it meet; `prompts-opening`'s raised ones come down only with step 4 or the prompt list's words kept.
- Step 4: the state file's frame after every fade is a whole paint only test windows make, counted in every budget for something that fades (`frames` one more, and its paint's cost, a millisecond for the tab menu, the most of `prompts-opening`'s difference). Publishing the settled state without a paint (the snapshot taken at the compositor's own wake, step 8 of the menu's flow, which already runs in every window), or counting that frame apart in `metrics`, would make the budgets measure Lachlan's window. It changes how every test reads `settled`, so it's a design of its own.
- The prompt list's 40 rows laid out and shaped on every paint it's in, in Lachlan's window too, wherever anything repaints while it's open (output in the pane below it): kept words, as render/words.rs keeps the other menus', is the redesign.

### A key while a menu is open

Since 2026-11-05 (stage 3 of landing 2); since 2026-11-07 (Lachlan's 7b) every key, bound ones too, but for the bindings an open menu acts on since 2026-11-08 (his 5a and 11a): a + menu row's shortcut and a paste into the picker's box. Traced from the code; nothing measured.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The raw passes (the key's physical code, then its raw code, then the key as the raw event has it, each looked up as key bindings, `process_key` with `OnlyKeyBindings::Yes`) don't run while a menu takes the keys (`lbterm_menu_takes_keys`: the tab menu, the prompt list, the Conductor's, the InMail list, the agenda, the context pill's list, the picker or its + menu, by their open state). A key down is asked of `lbterm_menus_binding` (step 2) by each of those three forms in turn, so a binding the open menu acts on is found by whichever form the key table keeps it by, as the passes would have found it; when one acts the key is marked handled, so no cooked event follows. Otherwise it returns without marking it, so Windows' key handling goes on to the cooked event (window/src/os/windows/window.rs `key`: a raw key not handled is translated and dispatched as a `KeyEvent`). Any other bound key (Ctrl+Shift+T's Reopen with no + menu open, Ctrl+W, Alt+S, a leader) does nothing here. Until 2026-11-07 they looked bindings up whatever was open, so a bound key acted over a menu (PITFALLS 150); from then until 2026-11-08 none was looked up, so the + menu's shortcuts did nothing over it (PITFALLS 185) | keyevent.rs `raw_key_event_impl` | every key | seven open checks (`lbterm_menu_takes_keys`, the context list's since the merge of 2026-11-07); with a key down, step 2's per form | yes |
| 2 | `lbterm_menus_binding` (since 2026-11-08): with the picker or its + menu the one taking the keys (they're last in step 3's order, so with any other menu open it answers false at once: those act on no binding), the key looked up in the default key table (`InputMap::lookup_key(key, mods, None)`: `normalize_shift` of the key with the positional modifiers gone, then a hash lookup), and the binding handed to `lbterm_new_tab_binding`. Over the + menu, `LbtermNewTab` 'Shell' or 'Reopen' named as the menu names keys (`key_name` of the normalized form, the very tuple the table holds, so the words are the hint's), and `newtab::plus_by_shortcut` picks the row that shows that key beside it: the first launch entry for Shell, Reopen's newest row for Reopen, only when it's there (not "Nothing closed yet") and its hint is that key (a second key bound to the same action isn't). Then `lbterm_plus_pick` as its letter would (step 4's pick, with its flash). Over the picker, Ctrl+V bound to `PasteFrom` (`PasteFrom 'Clipboard'` in his config) pastes into its box (step 8); since his 4b of 2026-11-08 Ctrl+V alone (`newtab::pastes_into_picker`, the key's words as above), so Ctrl+Shift+V and Shift+Insert, bound to a paste by the defaults, answer false as any other binding. Any other binding, or none, answers false: the key goes on to the menu (step 4) as a key no row takes, his 7b | render/menu_fade.rs `lbterm_menus_binding`, render/new_tab.rs `lbterm_new_tab_binding`, lbterm-extras newtab.rs `plus_by_shortcut` | per key down while the picker or the + menu is open: up to three from the raw pass and one from the cooked | a hash lookup a form; a key's name built only when its binding is one of the two | yes: it's how a row's shortcut and a paste reach the menu |
| 3 | The cooked pass: `process_key` with `OnlyKeyBindings::No` asks `lbterm_menu_takes_keys` before anything else, ahead of the leader's check and the bindings, and while one is open asks `lbterm_menus_binding` (step 2) for a key down, for a binding only the cooked form matches (one the raw pass didn't take), then gives the key down to the first open menu in order (`lbterm_menus_key`: each menu's `_key` answers false only when it isn't open) and a key up to nobody, then returns that it took it, so key_event_impl returns too: nothing reaches the pane, a modal, the Conductor's view or a key table, and no leader starts. Composed words (an IME's, a dead key's) go into the name the tab menu is renaming to or the picker's box, and anywhere else are a key no row takes (step 6). A menu fading out is closed already, its state gone at once (the menu's step 7), so every bound key acts again from the key after the one that closed it. Until 2026-11-07 each menu was asked in six blocks here and six more in key_event_impl's cooked branch, and composed words reached the pane under the tab menu | keyevent.rs `process_key`, render/menu_fade.rs `lbterm_menu_takes_keys`, `lbterm_menus_key`, render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_tab_menu_text`, render/new_tab.rs `lbterm_new_tab_text` | every key while one is open | one check a menu | yes |
| 4 | The key becomes a `menu::Key` (`menu_fade::menu_key`: a character only with no Ctrl, Alt or Windows key, a modifier alone `Modifier`), then the menu's answer: the tab menu's `menu::answer` (a `Did`), the + menu's letters (`newtab::plus_by_key`), the list menus' arrows, Home, End, Enter | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_tab_menu_key`, render/new_tab.rs `lbterm_plus_key`, render/prompt.rs, conductor.rs, inbox.rs, agenda.rs | per key | small | yes |
