| 7 | The frame hands the count over with the card (`MenuVisual::bumps`); `prepare_menu` sees it grew and `menu_lamp` makes the lamp the first time (a group and two visuals showing the slot's own swap chains over the card, for the hairline, and under the card a visual showing its surface through a colour matrix, for the light round it; the matrix made and set once, nothing drawn), places it when the hairline or the card's corner moved, shows the slot's swap chains again only when `MenuCard::chains_moved` says one may be new (a surface made, configured again for a new size by `Layer::place`, or the card's two surfaces swapped at a page turn by `menu_turn`), and plays `lbterm_motion::menu::bump` on both opacities from one DComp animation: up to the peak on `arrive_in`, back on `panel_glow`, from wherever it was (since 2026-11-08, Lachlan's 9a: the hairline all the way to white, and round the card its red light's alpha gone white at `palette::menu_bump_light_scale` times, white 50% at the card's edge; until then the hairline alone, 35% on `arrive_in` and back on `arrive_out`). Named `menu.<slot>.hairline`; with motion off nothing plays | compositor.rs `prepare_menu`, `menu_lamp` | per such key; then each frame of an open menu while the lamp exists, comparing the hairline and the corner | one animation set on two properties; the two `swap_chain` lookups (`as_hal`, then a QueryInterface each) only on a frame a chain may have changed, none on the others (PITFALLS 62); in DWM, while the flare plays (to `panel_glow`'s settling, about 0.6 s; about 0.3 s of it seen at real speed), the card's surface composed once more through the matrix, under the card, and the card's group composed as one layer as it is for its fades; at rest nothing: the lamp's two visuals are in the tree only from a flare's start to the first frame after its end (`MenuLamp::attached`, since the review of 2026-11-08; they had stayed in, at opacity 0 with the matrix on, from a slot's first flare on, a cost in DWM nobody measured), added and removed with one `AddVisual` or `RemoveVisual` each | yes |
| 8 | A paste into the picker's box (step 2's `PasteFrom`, his 11a): `lbterm_new_tab_paste` asks Windows for the clipboard's text (`window.get_clipboard`, the clipboard read off the GUI thread, as a pane's paste does in termwindow/clipboard.rs) and returns; when it comes, a `TermWindowNotif::Apply` back on the GUI thread puts it in if the picker is still open (`lbterm_new_tab_pasted`: nothing when it closed meanwhile): `newtab::pasted`, the first line with something in it, trimmed (the box holds one line), inserted over the selection as typed words (`Input::insert`: tabs as spaces, control characters left out), `edited` (the highlight back to the first row, the scroll to the top, "Started" gone), and an invalidate. With the state file on (a test window) the text is the state file's `copied` instead, what the window last copied, read at once, and the clipboard is never asked (PITFALLS 151). The next paint lists the projects for the new words (`base.listed`), as a typed key does | render/new_tab.rs `lbterm_new_tab_paste`, `lbterm_new_tab_pasted`; lbterm-extras newtab.rs `pasted` | per paste | one clipboard read off the thread, one notify, one paint | yes |
| 9 | The box drawn (each paint of the open picker): the part of the line measured and drawn, `from..to`, from `newtab::box_span` (2026-11-08): `from` the earliest start whose words up to the caret fit, `to` the first end past the caret whose words no longer fit (or the line's end), both found by galloping out from the caret (1, 2, 4... characters) and halving between the last span that fit and the first that didn't. Each probe is a measure (`lbterm_new_tab_advance`, two shapings kept by text) of a span no more than twice what the box holds, and the word handed to `compute_element` is `chars[from..to]`, at most the box and one character, so a paint's cost is bounded by the box wherever the caret is in however long a line. Its first fix (`box_from`) still measured the whole line before the caret first (`fits(0)`) and drew the line's whole tail (`chars[skip..]`), which `compute_element` shapes whole before it clips at `max_width`, so each Left press after a long paste shaped the line twice; before that it walked forward from the line's start, a measure of the rest of the line at each character (PITFALLS 192 and 209). The caret's x is the last span that fit, already kept | render/new_tab.rs `lbterm_picker_lay_out` (the box); lbterm-extras newtab.rs `box_span` | per paint of the open picker | a few times the log of what the box holds in measures, each of at most twice what it holds (nine for a box of ten part way into a line of 5,000, cargo-tested), plus the word of at most the box and one character; a line that fits with the caret at its end, as typing leaves it, takes about its length's log of short suffixes (four for seven characters), where it was one measure; kept by text, so a paint that changed nothing measures nothing new | yes |

What could go, or is open (not decided; for Lachlan):

- Step 1: a key bound in the config acted while a menu was open (PITFALLS 150). Done (2026-11-07, his 7b): the raw passes look nothing up while a menu takes the keys, and the cooked pass gives every key to the menu. A bound key over a menu flares its card's edge (since his 9a; it brightened the hairline until then) as any key no row takes does (`a_bound_key_over_the_tab_menu_is_a_key_no_row_takes`: Ctrl+Shift+R isn't Rename's R), except over the picker, which isn't one of the compositor's menu slots and has no lamp to flare: there, since his 3a of 2026-11-08, a key that neither moves, picks nor edits the box (a modifier on its own excepted) lights round the box (`lbterm_picker_light`, the `Light::Pad` Enter with nothing named brings; perf/new-tab.ps1 step 14). perf/menu-keys.ps1 step 8 holds it with a bound F9 (a posted key carries no Ctrl).
- Step 1's gate and the + menu's hints. Done (2026-11-08, his 5a): the + menu draws each row's bound key beside its letter (`newtab::plus_hint`: Ctrl+Alt+T on PowerShell 7 here, Ctrl+Shift+T on Reopen), and from 7b until then those keys over the open + menu only brightened its hairline (PITFALLS 185). Step 2 picks the row that shows the key; perf/menu-keys.ps1 steps 13 and 14 hold it with F6 and F7 bound to 'Shell' and 'Reopen' (a posted key carries no Ctrl), and F9, bound but shown beside no row, to the hairline. No other menu draws a bound key's name today: one that comes to has its row picked by step 2 only once `lbterm_menus_binding` asks it.
- Step 2 looks a key up in the default table only: an active key table (copy mode's, a leader's) isn't asked, as 7b bypasses them over a menu, and `locate_app_wide_key_assignment`, which names the hint, leaves out keys a table holds. A key pressed with the leader active is named without `LEADER`, so it picks the row as the key alone would.
- Steps 8 and 9: in a window without the state file, a paste key over the picker reads Lachlan's clipboard, as Ctrl+V into a pane does; a pane's paste in a test window (`PasteFrom` with no menu open) still reads the real clipboard, where every copy there goes to the state file (PITFALLS 151): reading `copied` there too would keep every test off his clipboard both ways, not asked so far.
- Step 7: `menu_lamp` asked for both swap chains every frame while the lamp existed, to see whether a surface's growth had made a new one (PITFALLS 62's trap, the review's finding). Since the fix-up, `MenuCard::chains_moved`, set where a chain can change (`Layer::new`, `Layer::place` returning that it was configured again, `menu_turn`'s swap), asks for them only then.
- The light (the menu's step 3): made again every paint the menu drew until the fix-up, which keeps it (render/kept.rs) by the card's place and size and the background; perf/menu-keys.ps1 step 3 holds a repaint of the open menu to building nothing of it.

### The tab menu's facts, and a pick from it

Since 2026-11-05 (stage 4 of landing 2). Traced from the code; no step measured on its own (each runs once a right-click or once a pick, never per frame or pointer move). Measured together: steps 1, 2, 6 and 7, all the release of a right click does, are WM_RBUTTONUP's own 0.19 ms with twelve tabs on the rail (the median of 50 runs, `budgets`' `tab-menu-opening`, against main's 0.02 for its link rows alone; "Opening, measured whole" above, step 2). All on the GUI thread.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The menu opens: the tab's link rows (`lbterm_link_menu`), its Claude session (`tab_session`: every pane's user vars copied, the active pane's `claude_session` first), its folder (`tab_folder`: `panel_tab` over the tab, which copies each pane's user vars and reads its session's summary from `crate::summaries`' cache, then the active pane's cwd, `AllowStale`, when the statusline gave none) and the project that's in (`lbterm_project_of`: `projects::Folders::of`, a pass over the config's projects, the `Folders` kept until the list changes) | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_open_tab_menu`, render/new_tab.rs, render/tab_panel.rs | per right-click on a tab | a pass over the tab's panes twice and over the projects once; no file read | yes: the rows are decided by them, once, never on a pointer move |
| 2 | The words measured for the card's width (`lbterm_menu_text`, `menu::widest` through `lbterm_new_tab_width`, kept by text, font and shape generation) | render/tab_menu.rs | per open or page turn | a shaping per row's words not yet kept | yes |
| 3 | Rename or Copy link picked: the label as the row shows it now (`tab_label`: `panel_tab` and `lbterm_panel::claude::row` for the one tab) | render/tab_panel.rs | per pick | one row's work | yes: the label can have changed since the menu opened (the summary worker writes it) |
| 4 | Copy link: `menu::copy_link`, then `copy_to_clipboard` (the clipboard, or with the state file on the state file's `copied`, `lbterm_copy_to_state`, as every copy in a test window), the pad's glint asked for (`lbterm_panel_glint_tab`, played by DWM next frame as `light.glint`) and the menu closed (its fade out, `pane_exit`) | render/tab_menu.rs | per pick | a string, one clipboard write, one paint | yes |
| 5 | New session: the menu closed, then `lbterm_new_tab_session_in`: the project again (`lbterm_project_of`), its counts for the event (`lbterm_projects_counts`: `panel_tab` and `lbterm_panel::claude::row` over every tab of every window, and the InMail counts as render/projects.rs keeps them), the Starting row asked for (`lbterm_new_tab_expect`), the project kept as a recent pick, first in memory and its file written by a thread of its own (`newtab::pick`, as the picker's pick does, Lachlan's 8b of 2026-11-07: the picker's next open lists it under recent), and `lbterm-project-action` fired through `lbterm_new_tab_fire` (a Lua event, async on the GUI's executor) with the tab's active pane | render/new_tab.rs | per pick | a row's work per tab open, then one Lua call | the counts: only for the event's `mail` and `sessions`, which Lachlan's handler doesn't read for `new` (What could go) |
| 6 | The rail's tabs, as the menu opens (stage 5): `tab_panel_menu_rail` walks `tab_panel_rail()` and, for each tab the last paint drew a row for (`drawn`, the panel's `lbterm_panel::claude::Tab` per tab, with its `seen` time and any tab link laid over), works out its row (`lbterm_panel::row`) once, as the rail draws it, link and all: its status word, unread dot, agents and tasks, its pad's colour (`palette::pad(state)`), its label, and `linked` (`drawn.link.is_some()`: a link the links file holds or one the GUI asked of it and draws already; either way the tab waits, so it isn't finished, PITFALLS 153). Stage 5 read the status without the link, citing PITFALLS 52, and Close finished tabs then closed a tab waiting on another; the review caught it. Then its session (`tab_session`) and its objectives' mark (`crate::summaries::get`, the in-memory cache, `.objectives.mark()`). `menu::Facts::from_rail` then picks Wait on…'s others and Close finished's tabs from that list, and `links_on` is `config.lbterm_links.is_some()` | render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_menu_rail`, render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_open_tab_menu` | per right-click | a row's work per rail tab, a user-vars copy per tab, one cache lookup per Claude tab; no file read. With the panel off nothing was drawn, so no tab is listed: no Wait on… and no Close finished there | yes: both rows and their words come from it, once |
| 7 | Close finished's second line, cut once as it opens: a layout for the card's `second` width, then `menu::finished_line` (`cut_to`'s halving: a few `lbterm_new_tab_width` measures, each kept by its words) into `Open::second`, which each paint draws at white 50% and the state file gives as the row's `detail` | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_menu_second` | per right-click, when a tab is finished | a few shapings the first time those words are seen | yes; never on a paint or a pointer move |
| 8 | Wait on… or ‹ Back picked (or Left, Esc on the page): the page turns (`Open::page`), its words measured again (step 2), and `lbterm_menu_turn` counts a turn in the slot (`SlotPaint::pages`) and invalidates. The next frame hands the count over (`MenuVisual::page`); `prepare_menu` sees it grew and `menu_turn` swaps the card's two surfaces (the other made the first time a page turns, the card's size): the frame the new page is drawn into is the other's, and the one holding the old page's last frame goes just over it, where it was, and fades out on `swap` (`menu.<slot>.swap`), never drawn into again (PITFALLS 147). With motion off, or without the compositor, the new page is just there | render/tab_menu.rs, render/menu_fade.rs, compositor.rs `menu_turn` | per page turn | the first turn in a slot makes one swapchain; after that, two visuals moved in the tree and one fade; the new page's quads as any paint's | yes |
| 9 | A tab on Wait on…'s page picked: its session read again (`tab_session` on the tab the page listed), then `lbterm_link_wait_on(waiter, on)`, the right drag's drop since 2026-11-05: the objective the waiter's wait names when it's on that tab (his E6), the pending change laid over the rail until the links file says it (`lbterm_link_fire`), and `lbterm-tab-link {action='toggle', waiter, on}` fired (a Lua event, async); the menu fades out | render/tab_menu.rs, tab_links.rs | per pick | one Lua call | yes |
| 10 | Close finished tabs picked: the menu closed, the rail read again (step 6), `menu::to_close` keeping only the tabs the row listed that still qualify (one that started working, asked something or got a new result since stays; one that finished since isn't taken unseen), then each in the rail's order marked (`lbterm_mark_tab`: kept to reopen, its close marks written) and removed (`lbterm_remove_tab`: the budget's CloseTab timed to the frame without it, and the last tab's place given to a new one while the Conductor's pad stays). All in one turn of the GUI, so the rail's next paint loses every row at once and the rail's own motion for a closed tab plays for each in one commit: each row fades where it was (`rail.row.exit`, `pane_exit`), the rows below slide up on one spring (`rail.row.slide`, `pane_layout`), and when the open tab was one the mux makes another active, its panes dissolving in (`pane.dissolve`, `tab_switch`). The marker (`sync_marker`, its move decided by `lbterm_motion::rail::marker`): `Mux::remove_tab` runs `fixup_active_tab_after_removal` in the same call, which keeps the index, so the open tab now is the one that slid into the closed one's place in the window's list, and with no closed tab above it its row ends in the very slot the closed row had. Since 2026-11-07 (Lachlan's 3b) the marker rides up with that row: `sync_rows` has already started the row's slide this frame, so `sync_marker` sees the last open row gone from the plan and the new open row's `RowVisual::dy` off its slot (a slot down, at rest), and drives the marker from the row's place and speed on the rows' own curve (`pane_layout`), the very motion the row plays, so the ring is on the row at every moment (`rail.marker`, ending as the row's `rail.row.slide` does). Before, the marker's glide on `focus_move` was from 0 to 0 and played nothing (`drive_off` with no distance names nothing): it waited in the slot while the new open row slid up under it, as main does for Ctrl+W (traced by the fix-up's probe on main's installed build and the branch's, 2026-11-05; PITFALLS 157). When the open row doesn't move (the last row closed, so the one above opens, its slot unchanged), it glides up to it on `focus_move` as before (`rail.marker`). A tab opened by hand while its row slides still glides from the last open row, which is still there. Ctrl+W, a middle click, Close tab and Close finished tabs all reach it the same way, since it's the rail's plan that's read, not the close | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_close_finished`, termwindow/last_tab.rs; compositor.rs `PanelLayers::sync_marker` | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_close_finished`, termwindow/last_tab.rs | per pick | a row's work per rail tab, then a close mark file and a mux removal per tab | yes |
| 11 | A middle click on a rail row (his 10a, stage 5): `tab_panel_mouse`'s `MousePress::Middle` arm, only on the row under the pointer (`hovered`), never on the Conductor's pad (`on_head`) or the + pad (`on_pad`), resolved to the tab the last paint drew on that row (`tab_panel_drawn_tab`, from `drawn_rows`, the rows' ids the paint keeps) and only while it's still in the window: after a close and before the next paint the window's list has another tab at that position, and this close asks nothing (PITFALLS 156). Then `lbterm_mark_tab` and `lbterm_remove_tab`, as the menu's Close tab, so Ctrl+Shift+T (`LbtermNewTab 'Reopen'`) brings it back. On the open tab the marker does as step 10 says: it rides up with the row that slides into the slot, or glides up when it was the last row | render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_mouse`, `tab_panel_drawn_tab` | per middle click | a close mark and one removal | yes |

What could go, or is open (not decided; for Lachlan):

- Step 5: the counts walk every tab to fill two fields the handler ignores for a new session; the picker hands them over because its rows already have them. Leaving them out of a tab menu's event (or reading them only for the actions that use them) would drop the walk. It's once a pick, so it's small.
- Steps 1 and 3 call `panel_tab` for the one tab twice over a menu's life (its folder, then its label); kept from the open, the label could be stale by the pick, so it's read again on purpose.
- Step 6 works out every rail tab's row again, which the rail's last paint already worked out (it keeps the inputs in `drawn`, and since the fix-up the rows' tab ids in `drawn_rows`, not the rows themselves). Keeping the rows beside `drawn` would drop it; it's once a right-click, and once more at a Close finished pick, so it's small.
- Step 10, the marker: done (2026-11-07, Lachlan's 3b, stage 4 of landing 2's follow-up). It waited in the slot while the new open row slid up under it, the ring over the fading row's place for the slide's time; it rides up with that row now, on the row's spring from the row's place (`lbterm_motion::rail::marker`, held by its cargo tests; tab-mouse.ps1 steps 15 and 17 hold it to ending as the row's slide does, step 18 to the glide when the last row closes). Seen on screen on 2026-11-07 (`motion/capture.ps1` at slowdown 5, `unlocked-ride`: three tabs, the second open by a posted click, then `kill-pane` on it): in the first frame after the close the ring and bar are on the third tab's pad where it still is, a slot below, while the closed row's own highlight fades where it was, and they ride up with that pad on its spring (its small overshoot included) to the second slot, settled by about 770 ms (150 ms real). So the ring steps down a slot at the close and then rides up: a look for Lachlan, not measured against a glide. A tab closed above the open one (`kill-pane` on a row above it) rides the same way, the open row sliding up under nothing.
- Step 8: a page turn's dissolve lays the old card over the new one, so where both draw their light (the 11 px round the card) and the ring's antialiased edges, the two add up while the old fades: about a third more light at the halfway mark, for `swap`'s length. Inside the card both are opaque and nothing adds up. Holding the old until the new is three quarters in, as the rail's rows do (TAB-PANEL.md), wouldn't help here, since the new page is whole from the first frame. Not seen on screen yet (the PC was locked, PITFALLS 148).

### A park, from the pick or the action to the rail and the published file

Since 2026-11-09 (Lachlan's 8a, feat/rail-landing-4 stage 3; NEW-TAB.md, "Landing 4"; MUX.md, "Parked tabs"). Traced from the code; perf/park.ps1 checks each end (the row's `parked`, the dimmed pad, the file) and none of it is timed: it runs once a park, by hand.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Park tab picked (P or a click; the menu closes on `pane_exit`), or `LbtermPark` performed (a key, or Lua's `perform_action` from an event the control pipe fires): `park::can_park` (the tab's active pane's domain: a `ClientDomain`'s `lbterm_can_park`, an atomic, else true), then `park::set_parked` | wezterm-gui park.rs, render/tab_menu.rs | per park | a few lookups, no I/O | yes |
| 2 | The GUI's own tab: `Tab::lbterm_set_parked`, an atomic swap, and on a change `Mux::notify(LbtermTabParked)` on the GUI thread, with no lock held. A mux domain's: `ClientDomain::lbterm_park` maps the tab to the server's and sends `TabParked` from a local task; the local tab isn't touched (PITFALLS 250) | mux tab.rs; wezterm-client domain.rs | per park | an atomic; or one PDU of a few bytes | yes |
| 3 | The server: `TabParked` read on the connection's task, set on the main thread (`spawn_into_main_thread`, `lbterm_set_parked`), UnitResponse back | wezterm-mux-server-impl sessionhandler.rs | per park | one task | yes |
| 4 | The server's notify: each connection's subscriber queues it as any notification; `notification_pdu` sends `TabParked` only on a connection whose client asked `GetParkedTabs` (`Pending::knows_parked`), the one that parked it included | dispatch.rs | per park, per connection | one small PDU each | yes: every window of the server shows the tab |
| 5 | The publisher's subscriber in the process that holds the tab (the server, or the GUI for its own tab): `may_change` true for a park, `DUE` set and one task queued on the main thread; it does nothing else, since `Mux::notify` holds its subscribers and a tab's own notify can come with the tab locked (`set_title`) | mux lbterm_parked.rs | per park; per pane added or gone, tab added or resized, window gone while the file names a pane | an atomic, a task | yes |
| 6 | `look` on the main thread: every window's tabs, an atomic load each, the panes of the parked ones (`iter_panes_ignoring_zoom`, filtered by `holds`: the GUI's own panes, not a mux domain's), sorted; compared with the last list and, when it differs, its text sent to the `lbterm-parked` thread, which takes the newest waiting and writes `<socket>.parked.json.tmp` then renames it over the file, retrying every 5 ms for up to 250 ms while a reader holds the file without FILE_SHARE_DELETE; a write that still fails is logged and sets the publisher's `last` to `None`, so the next look writes its list whatever it is (PITFALLS 256). A GUI's file stays while its socket does: upstream's sweep (every GUI's `update_checker`, 10 s after it starts and daily, AT-REST.md) counts only `gui-sock-<pid>` as a socket and takes the file once its socket has gone (`lbterm_beside_gui_sock`); before the review's fix-up it took the live GUI's own file at its first sweep (PITFALLS 254) | mux lbterm_parked.rs; wezterm-client discovery.rs | per step 5's task | a walk of the tabs (a few dozen), one small file written off the GUI thread | yes, only when the list changed |
| 7 | The window: `TabParked` handed on by the client thread by its kind (`process_unilateral`) through the backlog to the GUI thread (`process_unilateral_now`), `lbterm_remote_tab_parked` maps it to the local tab and sets it there (step 2's notify, in the GUI) | wezterm-client client.rs, domain.rs | per park, per window | an atomic and a notify | yes |
| 8 | The GUI's notify: the window subscriber passes it to the window holding the tab (`TabResized`'s test) and `lbterm_repaint::changed` gives `Changed::Tab`, so that window is invalidated; the window's own handler does nothing more | wezterm-gui termwindow/mod.rs; mux lbterm_repaint.rs | per park | an invalidate | yes |
| 9 | The paint: `panel_tab` loads the flag into `lbterm_panel::claude::Tab::parked` with the rest of the tab's row (an atomic load a row a paint), `row` copies it to `Row::parked`, which is in both looks, so the row's closed look changes (it dissolves on `swap`) and its words are laid out again; `tab_panel_pad` draws its colours blended 40% from the pill and `words::dim_parked` its number, label and mark, both into the rows' kept quads, made again only for this change; with the compositor a parked row is also left out of the plan's `spinners` (`Row::arc_turns`), so its arc is drawn still and dimmed in the body rather than placed from the undimmed turning template, and a row with tasks running that's parked takes its `rail.spinner` loop away (PITFALLS 255); its progress fills on the open panel are dimmed in their own kept quads | render/tab_panel.rs; lbterm-panel claude.rs, look.rs, words.rs | per paint (the load); per change (the rest) | an atomic load a row a paint; one remake of the rows' quads and words | yes |
| 10 | A window attaching, reconnecting or resyncing: at the end of `process_pane_list`, `lbterm_ask_parked` asks `GetParkedTabs` from a local task; the server notes the connection asked, then answers from the main thread (`parked_tabs`: every window's tabs, an atomic each); the answer sets `lbterm_parks` and every local tab of the server's from it, the tab map copied out first (a set notifies, and the domain's subscriber reads the map). An older server's error naming 7508 leaves the domain unable to park | wezterm-client domain.rs; sessionhandler.rs | per listing (attach, reconnect, every resync: a tab added or resized from elsewhere) | one round trip of a few bytes | yes: a tab parked before a listing mapped it had its push dropped |
| 11 | The file read (Terminal's, not LBTerm's): Claude Code runs the status line every 10 s and on each message of every session, `claudehook statusline` (Terminal's status line's first stage, since 2026-11-09) stats `${WEZTERM_UNIX_SOCKET}.parked.json` among the files whose modified times make its signature (`statusline::parked_file`), and only when the signature moved runs statusline.ts, whose `parkedHere` reads the file and looks for its `WEZTERM_PANE` in `panes` | Terminal's shared/claude/claudehook-rs (main.rs `statusline_verb`, statusline.rs) and statusline.ts | every status line run, each session | a stat a run; a read of a few bytes when the line is made again | yes: the box follows a park within a run, where a signature without the file (the old markers' paths) kept the old box until the minute or the transcript moved |

What could go, or is open (not decided; for Lachlan):

- Step 10 asks again on every resync, which upstream runs for every `TabAddedToWindow` and `TabResized` it hears; only a tab mapped by that listing can be out of step, so asking only when the listing mapped a tab it didn't have would do, at the cost of telling the two apart in `process_pane_list`. A few bytes a resync; left as it is.
- Step 6 walks every tab to find the parked ones; the count of parked tabs is small, but nothing keeps them apart from the rest. A few dozen atomic loads a look, and a look runs only while something is parked or one changes.
- The `lbterm-parked` thread is one more thread in every GUI and every server for a file written a few times a day; the write could be a task on a thread pool that's already there. It costs nothing while it waits on its channel (AT-REST.md), so it stays.

### A side button's hop, from the press to the tab switch

Since 2026-11-09 (Lachlan's 8a and 9a, feat/rail-landing-4 stage 4; NEW-TAB.md, "Landing 4", 9a). Traced from the code; perf/hops.ps1 checks where each press lands. Until then each hop was a Lua callback in his lbterm.lua that walked `tabs_with_info()` and opened a marker file a tab under `~/.claude/parked` on the GUI thread.

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | WM_XBUTTONDOWN (Back is XBUTTON1, Forward XBUTTON2) with `MK_SHIFT` in its wParam when Shift is held: `mouse_button` makes a `MousePress::Back` or `Forward` with the modifiers from the wParam, never `GetKeyState`, so a posted press carries Shift too, and answers TRUE so Windows sends no WM_APPCOMMAND | window/src/os/windows/window.rs | GUI | per press and per release | a message | yes |
| 2 | `lbterm_side_button`: a `Down { streak: 1 }` trigger with the event's modifiers, `lookup_mouse` (positional mods dropped), and `perform_key_assignment` with what's bound; the press is never passed to the pane, bound or not. The release looks up `Up`, which nothing binds | termwindow/mouseevent.rs | GUI | per press | a hash lookup | yes |
| 3 | `LbtermTab(how)` → `lbterm_tab_action` | termwindow/mod.rs, termwindow/hop.rs | GUI | per press | nothing | yes |
| 4 | `tab_panel_hops`: the mux window's tabs (`Mux::get_window`, a read lock), each one's `lbterm_parked()` (an atomic load), whether it's the Conductor's, and for a tab with a row drawn last (`lbterm_panel.drawn`) `lbterm_panel::waits_on_you`, which runs the row's whole `row_since` again at now (strings and all) to read its state and `since`; the open tab's index; `recent` copied out | render/tab_panel.rs | GUI | per press | a few microseconds for a dozen tabs, no I/O | the parked flags and the open tab, yes; the rows only for `'Waiting'` (below) |
| 5 | `hop::pick`: `'Back'`/`'Forward'` steps from the open tab, wrapping, past parked ones; `'Waiting'` sorts the waiting (`hop::waiting`) and takes the next after the open tab, or the first; `'Last'` the first of `recent` that's another tab still in the window and not parked | lbterm-extras hop.rs | GUI | per press | a sort of a dozen | yes |
| 6 | `activate_tab(index)`: the switch a click on a pad makes (CODEMAP.md, "A tab switch", from its step 2): the budget's `tab` action begins, `remember_and_set_active_tab_idx`, the new pane told `focus_changed(true)`, `update_title`, `update_scrollbar`; a press that goes nowhere stops at step 5 and paints nothing (logged at debug) | termwindow/mod.rs | GUI | per hop | as any tab switch | yes |
| 7 | The paint that shows the new tab: `tab_panel_tabs` sets its `seen` and puts it in front of `recent` (`hop::Recent::shown`: one compare while the front tab is the same; when it isn't, the tabs not in the window dropped by a walk of them and the tab put in front) | render/tab_panel.rs | GUI | per paint of the rail | a compare a paint; a walk of the tabs per switch | yes: it's what Shift+Back reads |

What could go, or is open (not decided; for Lachlan):

- Step 4 works out every drawn row again at the press for each of the four hows, where only `'Waiting'` reads them; asking for the rows only for it would save a few microseconds a press, which no one would see. Left as one gather, since the press is rare.
- `recent` and the rows are kept only while the rail paints (`lbterm_tab_panel.enabled`): with the rail off, `'Last'` and `'Waiting'` go nowhere. Lachlan's rail is on.

## The Conductor's state file, from its write to the paint

Since 2026-11-09 (stage 1 of New-tab landing 3, which reads the file's `hidden` here too). Traced from the code (wezterm-gui/src/state_files.rs, lbterm-extras/src/conductor.rs and watch.rs); no step measured on its own. It runs once a write of button.json (`@Conductor:button`): the Conductor writes it whenever its state, mode, listening, asks or hidden list change, on every `conductor hide` or `show` (already so or not), and when its seat starts with it off. A hide or a show is one such write, so Hide from Conductor adds no step and no thread.

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The Conductor writes a temporary file and renames it over `conductor/button/button.json`, alone in its folder since it moved there (AT-REST.md, "Busy folders") | the Conductor's conductor.ts | the Conductor's | per change of what it publishes | its own | yes |
| 2 | Windows' `ReadDirectoryChangesW` on the folder (and its parent, for the folder's own name) ends; LBTerm's copy of notify passes on only what's about a target, and `Watch`'s callback sends `Wake::Seen` down the thread's channel | deps/notify, lbterm-extras/src/watch.rs | notify's watcher thread | per rename (a temporary file made, written and renamed is several changes) | a wake of notify's thread a change | yes |
| 3 | `lbterm-state-files` wakes on the channel, keeps going only when `watch.matters(&paths)`, sleeps `SETTLE` (25 ms) so the writer's changes come as one, and drains what came meanwhile | state_files.rs `start` | lbterm-state-files | per write | a wake and a 25 ms sleep | the settle: yes, a write is several changes |
| 4 | `files()`: the config as it is now (`config::configuration()`, an `Arc`), and from it the Conductor's `state_file`, the links file and the agenda's | state_files.rs | lbterm-state-files | per wake | a config `Arc` and three strings cloned | yes: a config reload can name other files |
| 5 | `watch.follow(&targets)`, up to `FOLLOWS` (8) times while it changes something: the folders watched made right when one was made or removed | watch.rs `follow` | lbterm-state-files | per wake | nothing when the folders are as they were | yes |
| 6 | `look`: each Conductor state file's stamp (`std::fs::metadata`: modified time and size); one unchanged since it was last read is passed over. A changed one is read whole (`read_to_string`) and parsed (`conductor::read`: serde_json into a `Value`, its words, numbers and true or false kept as `Said::fields`, and since 2026-11-09 its `hidden` list as `Said::hidden`, a set of session ids, `None` without a list). A file that doesn't parse keeps what it said last. Then `SAID`'s lock, only to compare what each says with what it said (`Said`'s `==`, every field and the hidden set, `updatedAt` among them) and put it in as an `Arc` | state_files.rs `look`, conductor.rs `read`, `Hidden::read` | lbterm-state-files | per wake | a metadata call a file; for a changed one a read of a few hundred bytes (about 250 with none hidden, and about 40 more a session hidden, its 36-character id) and a parse | yes |
| 7 | `look_links` and `look_agenda`: their files' stamps, each passed over when unchanged | state_files.rs | lbterm-state-files | per wake | a metadata call each | yes, as the wake can be theirs |
| 8 | When anything said differs: `repaint_windows` sent to the GUI thread (`spawn_into_main_thread`, PITFALLS 108), which invalidates every window | state_files.rs | lbterm-state-files, then GUI | per write that changed what a file says: every write of button.json, since each has a new `updatedAt` | one task and an invalidate a window | doubtful: every window repaints for any write, though a hide changes what only the windows holding that session's tab show, and a rewrite in the same words (a hide of a session hidden already) changes nothing shown but `updatedAt` (What could go) |
| 9 | Each window's paint reads it again from memory: `lbterm_conductor_said` (render/conductor.rs; `state_files::said`, `SAID`'s lock, a lookup by path and an `Arc` clone, no file) for the pad (`lbterm_conductor_head`), for the Conductor's tab without a seat (`tab_panel_rail`, tab_panel.rs), for the view while it's open (conductor_view.rs), and for the pad's menu while it's open, where an item that waits is answered once what it says differs (`conductor::answered`, so a hide's write answers a waiting mode item too, as any write did before); since stage 2 of landing 3, for the rail's rows too (render/conductor_hide.rs: `lbterm_hides_settle` at the top of `paint_tab_panel` while a Hide or Show waits, and `lbterm_hide_looks` every paint, below) | render/conductor.rs, render/tab_panel.rs, render/conductor_view.rs, render/conductor_hide.rs | GUI | per paint of a window with the pad | a lock and an `Arc` clone a call | yes |

What could go (not decided):

- Step 8: every window repaints for every write, where a hide changes only what the windows holding that session's tab show (the tab menu's row and the mark) and nothing on the pad. Hides are rare (Lachlan's own picks), and the Conductor's other writes change the pad, which every window has; so it's small, and comparing what each window shows before invalidating it would cost more than the paints it saves.

### A Hide from Conductor pick, to the Conductor's word

Since 2026-11-09 (stage 2 of landing 3, with the look and the view's Show from its stage 3 the light up the rail from its stage 4 and Terminal's handler, step 3, from its stage 5; NEW-TAB.md, sections 8 and 9). Traced from the code (render/conductor_hide.rs, compositor.rs's `prepare_lights`, lbterm-motion's send.rs, render/tab_menu.rs, render/tab_panel.rs, render/conductor_view.rs, lbterm-extras/src/conductor/hide.rs, lbterm-extras/src/seat/layout.rs, lbterm-panel's words.rs and layout.rs); no step measured on its own: each runs once a menu open, a pick or a paint, and perf/conductor-hide.ps1 holds the order (the row in the frames after the key, the light up the rail once the card has gone, the glint after the write, the notice no sooner than 5 s). All on the GUI thread but the Conductor's own work and the file's reading (the flow above).

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The tab menu opens: besides the facts of "The tab menu's facts, and a pick from it" (step 1 there), `lbterm_hide_offer`: nothing on the Conductor's own tab; else the file as last read (`lbterm_conductor_said`, a lock and an `Arc` clone), every pane's `claude_session` (`tab_sessions`: each pane's user vars copied, each id once, the active pane's first) and `Picks::offer` (`conductor::offer` on the file's list; `Offer::Waiting` while a pick of the tab's still waits, a greyed row that picks nothing, `menu::inert`, so no second request races the first, PITFALLS 244; Lachlan's 11b of 2026-11-09) into `Facts::conductor`. Asked again only when a pick for the menu's tab begins or settles while it's open (`lbterm_menu_hide_again`, from `lbterm_hide_pick` and `lbterm_hides_settle`: a check of the open menu's tab, nothing more, when it's another's), the greyed row then coming alive in its place | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_open_tab_menu`, render/conductor_hide.rs | per right-click on a tab | a user-vars copy per pane; no file read | yes: the row and its words are decided by it, once |
| 2 | H, a click, or Enter on the row: the row flashes as any pick does, the label read again (`tab_label`), the menu closed (its fade, `pane_exit`), then `lbterm_conductor_hide`: the sessions again (`tab_sessions`), nothing more if a pick for the tab already waits (a menu opened before it began; logged, PITFALLS 244), the file's `updatedAt` kept in the `Pick` (`then`), the pick put in `TabPanelState::hides`, the window invalidated, and `lbterm-conductor-hide (window, pane, {sessions, label, hide, source})` fired (`source` "tab menu") with the tab's own active pane (a Lua event, async on the GUI's executor through `with_lua_config_on_main_thread`) | render/tab_menu.rs `lbterm_menu_pick`, render/conductor_hide.rs | per pick | a user-vars copy per pane, one Lua call | yes |
| 3 | Lachlan's config's handler (Terminal's lbterm.lua, `lbterm-conductor-hide`, built 2026-11-09 on Terminal's feat/conductor-hide), on the GUI thread inside step 2's Lua call: it returns nothing in a GUI with `LBTERM_STATE_FILE` (a test window, PITFALLS 68), so the event goes on to a test's own handler; else it words `--said` from `label`, `hide` and `source` ("picked Hide from Conductor on <label>'s tab in LBTerm", or "pressed Show on <label> in the Conductor's view in LBTerm"), matches each of `sessions` as a full session id (what isn't is logged and left out; each once) and calls `wezterm.background_child_process` once with `conductor hide` (or `show`) `<sid> [<sid>...] --by lbterm --said <his pick> --result <id>`, every id of the pick, and sets `await_result`'s first timer. One call a pick, never one a session: the processes would start side by side, never waited on, and the Conductor's `setHidden` reads hidden.json, changes its one id and writes it back with no lock, so two at once lose one with both results ok (PITFALLS 243; stage 5 built one a session, the review's fix-up made it one). Today's Conductor takes one id a call: two are a usage error with no result written, so a two-session tab's pick hides nothing, step 4 runs it out at 5 s and the notification says at 20 s that it didn't answer, until it ships several ids in one call (asked by LBTerm's InMail of 2026-11-09; NEW-TAB.md, section 8, "What the Conductor shipped"). `background_child_process` (lua-api-crates/spawn-funcs) is `smol::process::Command::spawn` with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and stdin null: the process is created on the GUI thread there and then, and never waited on. `wezterm.time.call_after` (lua-api-crates/time-funcs) is a task on the GUI's executor that sleeps on a `smol::Timer` and calls back through `emit_event` only if the config's generation is still the one it was set in (PITFALLS 241); each look is an `io.open` of `<id>.json` in `@Conductor:results`, at 4, 10 and 20 s after the pick until it's there, and deletes what it reads. The Conductor (its own Bun process) writes button.json again with a fresh `updatedAt`, hidden already or not, then its result; the flow above carries the write to every window's next paint, where step 4 settles the pick. A refusal or failure, or no result in 20 s, is one `window:toast_notification`, titled as `hide::not_done` words the strip's notice, with the reason; a call that worked says nothing. With no handler, or one that does nothing, nothing is written and step 4 runs the pick out | Terminal's lbterm/lbterm.lua (`lbterm-conductor-hide`, `await_result`), lua-api-crates/spawn-funcs, lua-api-crates/time-funcs; the Conductor | per pick; the looks one to three, the last 20 s after | one process created on the GUI thread (CreateProcess; not measured), one timer, up to three file opens. Off LBTerm's threads, but inside the 5 s step 4 waits for the write: Bun loading conductor.ts took 260, 297 and 318 ms here (`bun -e require(...)`, the module alone with nothing run, the PC idle, the review of 2026-11-09), then `setHidden` reads the session registry, walks the transcripts of the hidden sessions that aren't running (`gone`, for its prune) and of one being hidden that isn't, and writes two files: measured as a whole by the Conductor (its answer of 2026-11-09, merge 95d0f34): about 100 ms from the command's start to button.json's write, medians of 6 warm runs on a scratch LOCALAPPDATA with the real registry and transcripts (97 ms for one session, 103 for two, 98 for two with four sessions not running already on the list, its walk over 82 transcript folders adding nothing measurable), the process ending at about 115 ms, the PC lightly loaded; more under load and with a cold disk cache (the review's 260 to 318 ms was Bun loading the module alone, on a busier PC), and a pick that meets another's lock waits for its hold, a few ms. So the 5 s leaves well over 4 s for that work | yes: hiding is the Conductor's, and the result is the only place that says why one wasn't done |
| 4 | Every paint of the panel, first thing (panel on or off): `lbterm_hides_settle`, nothing at all while no pick waits. Else the file again (`lbterm_conductor_said`) and `Picks::settle`: a pick the file has been written since and `conductor::confirmed` says is done, its tab id pushed on `TabPanelState::glints` (no invalidate: this is the paint that plays it, `tab_panel_glints` after the rows, once no menu's card holds the rail); one 5 s old (`hide::PENDING`) goes and `lbterm_strip_notice` says `hide::not_done` for 4 s (an invalidate, the strip's wake at its end). Then `hide_due`, the first still waiting's end, which `tab_panel_wake` waits for with the panel's other times and its wake handler counts as due | render/conductor_hide.rs, render/tab_panel.rs `paint_tab_panel`, `tab_panel_wake` | per paint while a pick waits; per wake at a pick's end | a lock, an `Arc` clone and a set lookup a session per pick | yes; nothing polls: a write repaints every window anyway, and only the end wakes one |
| 5 | The rows: `lbterm_hide_looks` over the rail's tabs and the sessions `tab_panel_tabs` already gathered for the hub (none without `lbterm_conductor`), a `Look` a row: a waiting pick's `hide`, else hidden when the file's list names every session. When the file hides nothing and no pick waits, every row's at once without a lookup. Each look goes into `Tab::hidden` before the rows are made, so `Row::hidden` is in both of the row's looks (lbterm-panel's `look.rs`) and the row dissolves on `swap` when it changes, as `RowPlan` does for any look. With the state file on, `tab_panel_said` gives each row `hidden` and `hidden_pending` | render/conductor_hide.rs, render/tab_panel.rs | per paint of the panel | a lock and an `Arc` clone, then a set lookup per session only while something's hidden or waiting | yes: what the rail shows of it |
| 6 | The look drawn (stage 3): in `tab_panel_draw`, while any visible row is `hidden`, the marks' font (`tab_panel_mark_font`: the title font's style at 7.5 pt, 10 px at 96 DPI, kept in `TabPanelState::mark_font` by shape generation and DPI, so looked up once, `lbterm_sized_font` again only after a reload or a DPI change) and `words::marks`, a `Word` a hidden row (nf-md-eye_off, U+F0209, centred on `Layout::hidden_mark`, white at 60% over what's under the pad, `fill` or the card) into `Placed::marks`; `words::place` puts the "Hidden" chip after the project's (an `Area` in `Placed::chips`, its word white at 45% over `CHIP`, only when it fits whole). `tab_panel_pad` cuts the disc (`hidden_mark`, filled in what's under the pad) after the rings and the arc or unread dot, in the rows' quads (and again over an open row's highlight), so `rows_from` (the rows' looks hash, `hidden` in it) makes them again only when a row's look changes. The marks are laid out with the numbers, in the rows' z-layer (with the compositor `ROWS_Z`, so they move and dissolve with their row's visual), and again over an open highlighted row (`raised`); `WordsKey` holds the placed words and `mark_font`, so a frame that changes nothing lays out nothing | render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_draw`, `tab_panel_pad`, `tab_panel_mark_font`; lbterm-panel `words::marks`, `words::place`, `Layout::hidden_mark` | per paint of the panel; the font, the words and the quads only when a hidden row's look changes | with nothing hidden, one walk of the visible rows for `hidden`; with a row hidden, a `Rc` clone for the font, a `Word` and a disc a hidden row; the glyph shaped once (the words' cache, render/words.rs) | yes: the mark and the chip are what the hide shows |
| 7 | Show in the Conductor's view (stage 3): the Hidden group at the foot of Sessions (lbterm-extras's `seat::layout`, from `seat.view`'s `sessions[].hidden`; folded until a press, `Ui::is_folded` and `FOLDED_AT_FIRST`) gives each hidden session a Show button (`Target::Show`, its hit pushed after its row's, so the press is Show's, PITFALLS 237); a press runs `lbterm_view_show`: this window's tabs walked for one whose panes hold the session (`tab_sessions` a tab), then, found with a pick of its own still waiting, nothing more (logged: a second request would race the first, PITFALLS 244), found otherwise, a pick for that tab with that session alone (`lbterm_hide_pick`, so its row shows and waits as step 2's does) and its active pane, else no pick and the window's active tab's pane; `lbterm-conductor-hide` fired with `{sessions = {sid}, label, hide = false, source = "conductor view"}` (`lbterm_fire_conductor_hide`, the same Lua call as step 2's). The view stays; the seat's next `seat.view` without `hidden` moves the session out of the group | render/conductor_view.rs `lbterm_view_press`, render/conductor_hide.rs `lbterm_view_show`; lbterm-extras `seat/layout.rs` | per press | a user-vars copy per pane of the window's tabs, one Lua call | yes |
| 8 | The light sent up the rail (stage 4, Lachlan's 6a of 2026-11-01): `lbterm_hide_pick` (steps 2 and 7) pushes the tab on `TabPanelState::sends`, once a tab while it waits and only with the compositor and the panel on (`lbterm_tab_panel.enabled`: nothing else would take it off). A paint with the panel off (its early return in `paint_tab_panel`, after step 4's settle) or without the compositor clears `sends` and `glints` both, the glint step 4 pushed for a pick from the tab bar's menu included: only the composited panel's paint plays them, and before the review's fix-up they stayed queued, de-duplicated, until a reload brought the panel back and then all played at once, stale. The panel's paint, after the rows are drawn, in `tab_panel_glints` once no menu's card holds the rail (`hover.held()`, his 7b: a pick from the tab menu waits out the card's `pane_exit`, so it sets off in the frame the row dissolves into its new look), runs `tab_panel_sends`: the list taken, and for each tab with a row in view on a rail with the Conductor's pad (`layout.head`), `Light::Send` from its pad's glint rectangle (the pad grown 2 px, 6 px corners) to the Conductor's, put on this frame's lights (`lbterm_light_this_frame`: no frame of its own). The compositor's `prepare_lights`, after the paint pass: `lbterm_motion::send` plans it (`None` with motion off), a `Lit` from the lights' pool shows the white swatch through a clip the glint's size, its offset Y driven from the tab's pad to the Conductor's on `send` (X too, which stays: every pad is in the rail's column, the same size), its opacity's segments (up to 35% on `copy_in`, a constant cubic held to the arrival, out on `copy_out` from there) played, the visual added to `panel_lights` and `shown` until its end; the first `prepare_lights` after that, whenever a frame next comes, puts it back in the pool | render/conductor_hide.rs `lbterm_hide_pick`, render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_sends`, compositor.rs `prepare_lights` (`Light::Send`), lbterm-motion's send.rs | once a pick (two picks of one tab under one card send one) | a `Vec` push at the pick; at its frame a position lookup, one plan (cubics a 120th of a second apart: about 72 for the climb and 55 for the light) and three DComp animations (two named `light.send`; X, not moving, is set). Then nothing while it plays: DWM carries it for 860 ms, no frame is asked for and the GUI thread runs nothing for it; its visual stays in the tree at nothing until the next frame after its end | yes: it's the light he asked for (6a); nothing of it runs at rest, per frame or with motion off |

What could go (not decided):

- Step 1 and step 2 each copy every pane's user vars for the sessions (and step 1's other facts copy them again, `tab_session`, `tab_folder`); the paint's `tab_panel_tabs` already has each rail tab's sessions. Keeping them beside `drawn` would drop the copies; it's once a right-click and once a pick, so it's small. Measured whole (2026-11-09, stage 6 of landing 3; PERF.md, "Hide from Conductor, by budgets -Against"): the opening with the Hide row on a twelve-tab rail, `tab-menu-hide-opening`, took 1.43 ms against main's 1.53 to the frame that shows it and 2.92 ms of the GUI thread against 2.98, the same within the rounds' spread (±0.38 and ±0.74), so the copies aren't worth taking out for the opening's sake.
- Step 3 creates the pick's process on the GUI thread (`background_child_process` spawns where it's called, as every handler's start in Terminal's lbterm.lua does); once a pick, so it stays, but a thread for the spawn would take it off the thread that takes his keys. Its result's timers are lost to a config reload in the 20 s after the pick (PITFALLS 241): the hide still happens and the strip still says one that wasn't done, but its reason never comes.
- Step 4 asks the file even when nothing was written since the last paint; a lock and an `Arc` clone, only while a pick waits (5 s at most), so it isn't worth a generation counter.
- Step 6 walks the visible rows for `hidden` every paint of the panel to decide whether the marks' font is wanted; the rows are a few dozen at most and the walk stops at the first hidden one, so it stays.
- Step 7 copies every pane's user vars of every tab in the window to find the session's tab, as `lbterm_view_open_session` does for a session's card; the paint's `tab_panel_tabs` has the rail's sessions already. Once a press; small.
- Step 8's light is planned and handed to DWM in the frame it sets off, then costs the GUI thread nothing; its visual waits in `panel_lights` at nothing until a frame next comes after its end, which may be long after when the window is idle. That's how every light is put away (`prepare_lights`), and a visual at nothing costs DWM a tree node, not a frame, so it stays.

### The hub's word of what the Conductor is on, to the outlines

Since 2026-11-09 (landing 4's stage 2, Lachlan's 7a: the Conductor's pad lights only while it acts; NEW-TAB.md, "Landing 4"). Traced from the code (wezterm-gui/src/conductor_hub.rs, render/tab_panel.rs, compositor.rs's `sync_rings`, lbterm-extras/src/conductor.rs's `Focus`, `outline`, `PadRing`); no step measured on its own. It runs once a `conductor.focus` line: the Conductor's hub sends its whole list of the sessions it's thinking about or acting on (`doing`, an optional `until` in epoch ms) whenever that list changes. Its estimates (`conductor.estimates`, the progress bars) come down the same pipe and go through `set_estimates` instead, not traced here.

| # | Step | Where | Thread | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `listen`'s `read_line` on the hub's pipe (`hub_pipe`, `\\.\pipe\conductor-hub`) returns a line; `conductor::focus_message` parses it (serde_json) into `Vec<Named>`. The thread started the first time a paint asked `doing`, `acting` or `estimate` (`START`), and reconnects with a backoff when the hub goes, clearing everything (`set_focus(None)`) | conductor_hub.rs `start`, `listen` | lbterm-hub | per line | a parse of a few hundred bytes | yes |
| 2 | `set_focus`: `FOCUS`'s lock; `Focus::next_change` before; `Focus::set` (each named session shown from now or carried on with its new `until`, each one not named marked `gone` now, those past their end pruned), true when what shows changed (a session added, its `doing` changed, one pruned); `next_change` after | conductor_hub.rs, conductor.rs `Focus::set` | lbterm-hub | per line | a few map entries | yes |
| 3a | Changed: `repaint_windows` sent to the GUI thread (`spawn_into_main_thread`), every window invalidated | conductor_hub.rs, state_files.rs `repaint_windows` | lbterm-hub, then GUI | per line that changes what shows: an outline starting or changing kind, which since landing 4 can light the Conductor's pad in every window | one task and an invalidate a window | yes: the pad is in every window |
| 3b | Not changed but its ends moved (since 2026-11-09, PITFALLS 249): the hub let go of a session, which still shows for its least lap (`ACTING_AT_LEAST`, 1.4 s; `THINKING_AT_LEAST`, 600 ms) and its linger (`LINGER`, 400 ms), or named one again with a new `until`. A task on the GUI thread notifies every window (`TermWindowNotif::Apply`, a second hop, PITFALLS 81) with `tab_panel_hub_wake`: nothing without a rail laid out; else `due.1` (the hub's due) set to `next_change` and `tab_panel_wake` for it. No paint now. Until 2026-11-09 nothing ran here, and an outline let go of stayed until some other paint | conductor_hub.rs `set_focus`, render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_hub_wake` | lbterm-hub, then GUI | per let-go or new `until` | two tasks and a timer a window | yes: nothing else knows the end |
| 3c | Neither: nothing | | | | | |
| 4 | The paint: `paint_tab_panel` asks `conductor_hub::doing` for each rail tab's sessions (a lock and a lookup each) and, since landing 4, `conductor_hub::acting` once (`Focus::acting`: any entry acting and not past its end, here or not) into `TabPanelState::head_acting`, only with a hub configured and the pad on the rail. `tab_panel_rings` takes the pad's outline (`conductor::outline(level, hovered, acting)`: `spectrum` in Accept most and all only while acting or under the pointer) through `PadRing::at`, which keeps the pad's spectrum named in the plan, lit or fading, until a fade out on `panel_glow` would have settled (`compositor::fade_out_lasts`: `panel_glow`'s spring from 1 to within 0.002 of 0, about 540 ms at real speed, worked out from the spring, not measured) and sets `ring_due` for then; each tab acted on or thought about gets its iridescent ring and light. With the state file, the pad's `outline` and `acting`. Then `due.1 = next_change()` (pruning what's gone) and `tab_panel_wake` for the soonest of the panel's dues, `ring_due` among them | render/tab_panel.rs `paint_tab_panel`, `tab_panel_rings`, `tab_panel_head_outline`; conductor.rs `outline`, `PadRing` | GUI | per paint of a window with the rail | a lock a session and one more for `acting`, a few comparisons | yes |
| 5 | The frame's compositor pass: `sync_rings` compares the plan's rings with the visuals it has; the same (an idle repaint) does nothing. Otherwise a ring kept (`RingPlan::same_ring`: the same place, style and light, lit or fading) keeps its visual and its hues where they are, and the pad's fades to 1 or 0 from wherever it is (`Ring::fade_to`, a DComp opacity animation on the plan's curve, `conductor.outline.fade` in the state file's animations); a new one is made (its visual, a hue rotation DWM turns once every 6 s while `spin`, `conductor.outline` in `loops`), the pad's from 0, and one the plan no longer names is removed at once | compositor.rs `sync_rings`, `new_ring`, `Ring::fade_to` | GUI | per frame whose plan's rings changed | a visual or two and an animation; then DWM carries the fade and the hues, nothing on the GUI thread | yes |
| 6 | The wake: at the outline's end, the timer's task on the GUI thread (`tab_panel_wake`'s handler) asks `changes_by(now)` and invalidates; the paint (step 4) finds the outline gone, and for the pad's spectrum in Accept most and all starts its fade out. At `ring_due`, a paint takes the faded ring out of the plan, and the frame removes its visual | render/tab_panel.rs `tab_panel_wake` | GUI | per outline's end, and once more per fade out of the pad's ring | a paint each | yes |

At rest in Accept most or Accept all nothing in this flow runs: no line comes, no wake is set for the hub, and no ring is named, so DWM has no loop of the Conductor's to compose (before landing 4 the pad's spectrum turned there for good, PITFALLS 36).

What could go (not decided):

- Step 3a invalidates every window for any change, and step 3b notifies every window for any let-go. Before landing 4 only the windows holding a session's tab showed its outline; now the pad's spectrum is in every window, so a change of acting needs them all, but a thinking change still repaints windows that show nothing of it. Lines come as often as the Conductor acts, a few a minute at most when it's busy: small next to a frame's cost.

## A frame, from the invalidate to DWM's present

Not traced as rows yet. CODEMAP.md's "A frame" has the path, COMPOSITOR.md's "How a frame reaches the screen" the compositor's part, and these found what a frame did that it needn't:

- perf/frame-loop (2026-10-30): the GUI thread spun between frames, since a held `WM_PAINT` left its region invalid and Windows sent it again at once. A held paint is now validated and a high-resolution timer ends the hold, and the message loop waits with no timeout (`window/src/spawn.rs`, `run_until_message`; CODEMAP.md, "A frame", step 2). Frames come when they're due: a period from a paint's start, `max_fps`' 8.3 ms or the screen's refresh if that's longer.
- perf/lbterm-layers (2026-10-31): LBTerm's own drawing laid its cards', lists' and rail's words out again every frame; now it does so only when what they show changes, kept by `kept.rs` and `render/words.rs` (a frame with the InMail list open over a busy tab, 47 to 4.6 ms of the GUI thread; with the projects card, 18 to 4.6). PERF.md, "What the cards cost".
- perf/paint (2026-11-01): a frame keeps what didn't change. The card, ring, glow and lit-ring templates are built once each time what they're made from changes (`render/kept.rs`), each layer keeps one GPU buffer and only the quads whose hashes differ are written to it (`termwindow/gpu_work.rs`, `WebGpuVertexBuffer::upload`; `lbterm_motion::content::dirty_runs`). COMPOSITOR.md's "What a frame costs" paragraph.
- perf/lbterm-layers too: every line a pane draws went past LBTerm's own looks at it (the triggers' rules, the thumbnails' look for a picture's link, the link under the pointer) before its cached quads could answer; now a line is laid out again only when it changed (PERF.md, "What a line costs"; the state file's `work.lines` counts the misses).
- Work on a pane's rows stays out of `with_lines_mut`, which holds its terminal against its parser: copy what's needed (`render/pane_lines.rs`) or read what's cheap (PERF.md, findings 37 and 69; CODEMAP.md, "Hot paths").
- Frames missed while motion plays: PERF.md, "Frames missed during motion".

Not known yet: a whole busy frame's steps with each one's cost, the GPU driver's part, and what DWM does between the present and the screen.

## A test window's motion, from an episode's opening to the budget's `missed`

How a test window says whether every refresh of its screen got a new frame while its motion played, from the frame that starts the motion to the budget runner's verdict on `missed` (PERF.md, "Frames missed during motion"; the budget of 0 on every action). Off in Lachlan's window: all of it runs only while `lbterm_extras::present` is on (`LBTERM_PRESENT_TIMING` in a test window's environment, which the budget runner gives every action, or a `metrics --present on` request). Traced on 2026-11-08 from the code, a trace of the walk (`budgets.ps1 -Scenario tab-menu-opening -Runs 6 -Log 'info,lbterm::present=debug,lbterm::present::walk=trace'` and `rail-opening` the same, on the branch's build with the trace and on the installed main's; the right-most screen was covered every time, so no episode was walked), and every screen's counts read frame by frame (perf/screen-counts.ps1, 20 s, no test window open). All on the GUI thread but step 9, which is the runner's.

| # | Step | Where | How often | Cost | Needs to run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A frame's end hands over what the compositor has in flight (each animation's name and end), and the first frame with anything still to end opens an episode; later frames extend its names and its settle (`Episode::extend`) and count its paints and those over a refresh (`Episode::paint`) | wezterm-gui/src/present.rs `frame_end`, `Present::motion` | every frame while present timing is on; an opening an episode | a map lookup and a loop over what's in flight, a frame | yes, for the budget's motion figures |
| 2 | The screen's compositor target, once a monitor: the window's monitor (`MonitorFromWindow`, nearest), its adapter and VidPn source (`source_of`: `GetMonitorInfoW`, `D3DKMTOpenAdapterFromGdiDisplayName`), and the target among the newest completed frame's (`DCompositionGetStatistics`) with that display adapter and source, with one refresh of it (`vblankDuration`); kept by monitor handle for the process's life. This PC's four screens are sources 0 to 3 of one adapter, a target each; DISPLAY1, the right-most portrait screen where the test windows open, is source 0 | `Present::screen` | once a monitor a process | a few system calls, once | yes |
| 3 | Whether others cover the window: the windows above it in the z-order, sampled at 16 by 16 points of its part on its screen, at most every 250 ms; 95% covered is hidden, and a covered episode is never walked (`covered`), so it measures no `missed` | `Present::hidden`, `covered` | once an episode opening | a z-order walk | yes: a covered window's screen gets no frame of its motion, so every refresh would read as missed (PERF.md, "Hidden windows") |
| 4 | Its base: the newest completed compositor frame (`DCompositionGetFrameId`, `COMPOSITION_FRAME_ID_COMPLETED`) and the screen's counts as of it (`Clock::sample`: `DCompositionGetStatistics` and `DCompositionGetTargetStatistics`), and the walk made with the vblank as its refresh (`Walk::new`, none without one) | `Present::motion`, `Clock::sample` | once an episode | three calls into dcomp.dll | yes |
| 5 | Its looks: one timer for the soonest of the settle + 30 ms, a refresh apart after that until the walk's done or 200 ms have gone, and every 800 ms while it plays (the menu's "Opening, measured whole", step 6) | `Present::arm`, `wake`, `walk_all`; lbterm-extras `Episode::next_look`, `finished` | one look an episode while the PC composes, up to a dozen when it doesn't | a poll and a walk each | yes |
| 6 | A walk: every compositor frame since the last walked, to the newest completed (at most 240 back; further, it carries on from there, `skip_to`, and says `partial`), each read with `Clock::sample` and stepped (`Walk::step`). The compositor makes a frame a tick of the fastest screen's refresh only while something on some screen changes: 2,421 frames in 20 s with Lachlan at the PC (11:45), 845 in 20 s and 14 in 2.4 s when the PC was quieter (11:28) | `walk`, `Clock::sample` | per look, over the frames since the last | two calls into dcomp.dll a frame; metrics' `present.cost` (`walks`, `walk_ms`) | yes |
| 7 | Each frame stepped: before the first whose present count (`presentedStats.presentCount`: every present DWM made to that screen, whoever's) has moved from the base's, nothing is counted; that frame is where the motion went up (`first_ms` from its vblank time, `lead` in refreshes since the base). From it each frame adds the refreshes since the frame before by the screen's vblank times (`refreshes_between`: the `presentedStats.time` difference over the vblank, rounded) and the presents by the count; `missed` is the whole span's refreshes less its presents (a frame's counts can come a frame late), and a frame begun after the settle ends the walk. The screen's own refresh counter (`presentedStats.refreshCount`) is added up beside them as `counted`, judged by nothing. **Until 2026-11-08 the refreshes were the counter's**, which add up to the counter at the last frame less the counter at the first present, and the counter jumps at a present on this PC's screens (forward by thousands in every unlocked run of 2026-11-07, back by 4 to 61 on 2026-11-08) while the times stay a refresh apart: every `missed` judged on 2026-11-07 was the counter's jump, not frames DWM dropped (PITFALLS 166) | lbterm-extras/src/present.rs `Walk::step`, `refreshes_between` | per frame of the episode | arithmetic | yes |
| 8 | Finished (`Episode::finished`): what it came to (`Episode::done`, `MotionDone`), its line logged under `lbterm::present` at debug (in the form "motion menu.tab, 222 ms: 15 refreshes, 2 missed, first frame up at 4.3 ms; ...", "; the screen's refresh counter said N" when the counter disagrees, or "not measured, another window was over it"), added to the totals (`Totals::motion`: the measured episodes' refreshes, `counted`, presents and missed, and by name) | `Present::finish`; lbterm-extras `Totals::motion` | once an episode | a line when logged | yes |
| 9 | The runner: each run's `metrics` reply (reset before it) under `present.motion`; `ConvertTo-ActionSample` takes `missed`, `refreshes`, `refreshes_counted` and `motion_first_ms` only when an episode was measured (`measured` 1 or more); `Test-BudgetScenario` holds the runs' median `missed` to its budget, 0, and a metric no run measured is "nothing measured (not judged)" (PITFALLS 182) | lbterm/budgets.psm1 | per run, per scenario | none in the window | yes |

The trace, with `lbterm::present::walk` at trace in the window's log (wezterm-gui/src/present.rs): each target step 2 finds with the monitor's adapter and source, each episode's opening with its base frame and whether it was covered, every frame step 6 reads with its raw counts (the frame's start, target time and period; the screen's outstanding presents, present time and vblank; `presentedStats`' and `completedStats`' present count, refresh counter, virtual refresh counter and time), and each walk's span and running totals. With `lbterm::present` at debug, step 8's line.

What every screen's counts do, frame by frame (perf/screen-counts.ps1, 2026-11-08): `presentCount` moves by one a present on every screen; `time` moves on at every refresh DWM sees, a present or not, a refresh apart (within a fraction of a millisecond); `refreshCount` agrees with `time` on the main screen (DISPLAY2, 160 Hz, all 2,421 frames), lags it by one at each present on DISPLAY3, goes back 4 to 61 at 18 frames in 20 s on DISPLAY4 (393 refreshes by it against 1,200 by its times), and went back 39 at a present after a quiet spell on DISPLAY1, where the test windows open. A frame with a present outstanding (`outstandingPresents` 1) can carry a `refreshCount` ahead of the screen's, which the next frame takes back. `completedStats` is filled only at a frame where a present completed.

What could go, or is open (not decided; for Lachlan):

- The walk by times on a test window that can be seen, before and after: not measured. On 2026-11-08 the right-most screen was covered (DashboardsBox and Discord, 100% of it), so every episode was `covered` and `missed` unjudged, as in 48 of the 50 rounds of 2026-11-07 23:05 (PITFALLS 182). Uncovered, `budgets -Only tab-menu-opening,prompts-opening,inbox-opening -Against <main's build>` says whether the menus' fades miss refreshes, with `refreshes_counted` beside `refreshes` to show the counter's jumps; main's build, which still counts by the counter, can't be judged on `missed` against it.
- What made the right-most screen's counter jump forward from 2026-11-07 (it hadn't in any run kept from 2026-10-30 to 11-04) isn't known: not LBTerm's code (main's installed build jumped too); a display mode, driver or screen change that week would. Nothing here turns on it now.
- Steps 2 to 7 run only in test windows, a few system calls a frame of motion; none of them does work it doesn't need.
- An action's time to the screen (`screen_ms`) is read from another counter, DXGI's frame statistics for the swapchain that shows it (`lbterm_extras::present::seen`: the sync refresh's time, less the refreshes from the present's refresh to the sync's, a refresh each). If those refresh counts jump at a present as `presentedStats.refreshCount` does, a screen time would be off by the jump; not looked at frame by frame, but not by thousands: the 38 screen times of perf/mux-client's `budgets -Against` of 2026-11-07 20:13, on the screen and the day the counter jumped by thousands, came 12.8 to 26.3 ms after their presents, one or two refreshes.
